# Decisions (marketplace API)

Orchestrator decisions use ids `O-nn`; workstream decisions use `M1-nn`, `M2-nn`, ...

| Id | Date | Decision | Why |
| W3-06 | 2026-11-23 | **W3 follow-up (orchestrator): an account made by phone deletes itself on the web.** W3-02 left it out (the delete asks for a password, and such an account has none). (1) **Proof:** a recent sign-in (10 minutes, as for every sensitive change; a phone step-up makes it recent) AND a code texted for exactly this purpose, typed now: `POST /account/delete/phone/start` texts the account's own proven number (the body names none) a code of the new purpose `delete-account`, then `POST /account/delete/phone` deletes. The code is `PhoneSignInService`'s: an HMAC under `Auth:PhoneCodeKey` with the purpose in it, six digits, ten minutes, five tries counted before the compare, single use, the same per-number, global and per-address caps, and sent after the answer. A sign-in or step-up code never deletes, and a delete code never signs in or steps up (the purpose is in the HMAC and the row). The SMS says plainly that it DELETES the account and to ignore it otherwise. (2) **Only for an account without a password**, checked at start, at verify, and in the delete's own UPDATE (`PasswordHash` null and the same session epoch), so a password set in between stops it (`409 password_required`); an account with a password deletes with it. (3) **Same semantics as the password delete:** both paths share one core (soft delete, the address freed and handed to the deletion hooks, saved cards removed, every session revoked, the redaction queued in the same transaction). (4) **Audit:** every buyer self-delete, by password or by code, writes a `buyer.self_delete` row to the staff audit log in the same transaction (actor role `buyer`, target the account id, detail `{ method, sessionId }`): ids only, never the address or the number. **The row survives the account's erasure:** `RedactAsync` and the `IAccountEraser`s never touch the staff audit log (it holds no personal data), and only the audit log's own retention (`Staff:AuditRetentionDays`, at least 30) removes it (tested: the row is still there after the redaction). Tested in `PhoneAccountDeleteTests`. | Orchestrator follow-up 2026-11-23 to W3 item 2: a buyer must be able to delete an account they made by phone. |
| W3-05 | 2026-11-23 | **W3 item 5: the real-Stripe CI job becomes runnable and covers 3DS and webhook delivery (closes the M8-09 follow-ups).** (1) **What the owner adds:** the repository secrets `STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY` (`sk_test_`, or `rk_test_` with PaymentIntents and Refunds write and Events read) and `STRIPE_TEST_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` (`pk_test_` of the same account), listed in the README; without the first the job is skipped with a notice (unchanged), without the second only the 3DS step is skipped with a notice. (2) **Webhook delivery is a signed replay of Stripe's own event:** after the payment the job reads `payment_intent.succeeded` for that intent back from Stripe's API (`GET /v1/events`, filtered by type and time) and posts that body byte for byte to `/api/stripe/webhooks`, signed with the job's webhook secret exactly as Stripe signs; it delivers it twice and checks `stripe_events` holds it once. Chosen over the Stripe CLI (`stripe listen`): the CLI is a binary to download and pin on every run, and this session could not read its release facts (the network blocks Stripe's and the CLI's hosts; the orchestrator was asked to confirm the API facts used); the replay tests the receiver with Stripe's real event shape, and the transport itself (Stripe to our public URL) is checked by the deployment, not by CI. (3) **3DS:** with the publishable key the stack does NOT install the stand-in Stripe.js (the web loads the real one from js.stripe.com with the API's real `pk_test_`), and a browser signs in, checks out, types Stripe's 3DS test card `4000 0025 0000 3155` into the Payment Element's frames, presses Pay, completes the test challenge, reaches the confirmation page; the intent is read from Stripe (`succeeded`), its event replayed signed, and the order must reach the seller portal; any 5xx or page error fails it. The job installs Chromium for this. The web's CSP already allows Stripe's frames (`js.stripe.com`, `hooks.stripe.com`). (4) **Not run yet, and the Stripe facts are UNVERIFIED (from memory):** no key was available to the worker and neither the worker's nor the orchestrator's network could read Stripe's documentation, so these are assumptions to check on the first real run: A1 the Events API lists `payment_intent.succeeded` for the intent within seconds; A2 `4000 0025 0000 3155` always asks for 3DS and the test challenge has a "Complete" button in a Stripe frame; A3 the Payment Element's inputs are named `number`, `expiry`, `cvc`, `postalCode`; A4 no dashboard webhook endpoint is needed; also the `rk_test_` permissions listed in the README. A failure caused by one of them says "ASSUMPTION An". (5) **Where it runs (orchestrator decision):** a workflow of its own, `stripe-journey.yml`, on `workflow_dispatch` and a weekly schedule only, NOT on push or pull request, until one manual run has passed with the owner's real test keys (an unverified job that fails must not turn CI red and block every merge); then a follow-up may move it next to the journey job. The README recommends `sk_test_` for the first run. | Owner decision 2026-11-23 (W3 brief, item 5). |

Anything the code cannot answer (host names, network rules, certificates, SMTP provider, relay domain, PostgreSQL version and backups, tax rates, going live) is marked **owner to decide**, never guessed. `Auth__PhoneCodeKey` (W3-02) is listed as a new owner secret. The README's configuration table gains the `Stripe__*` rows and a link to DEPLOY.md. **Phone ordering and Sola (section 8, added after C19 landed):** every API and pay-connector setting, with its secret status and length rule, comes from the settings list of the worker who owns voice and payments (V-49, V-50), and each name was checked against the code. The four values two services must share are marked as **EQUAL pairs**: connector Basic user, password and shared key to `Sola__PayConnectorUser`/`Password`/`Key`, and the connector's SignalWire signing key to `SignalWire__SigningKey`. Section 8 also covers the outside steps: SignalWire `<Pay>`, the Sola webhook, the portal's Sola migration (the portal's side), and the `Sola: mode unknown` start-up line. Test keys only. **Follow-up (orchestrator, after W3):** section 1 now draws the pay connector as a third public host, its own service and never routed through the website or the API's host. It shows who calls it (SignalWire `POST /connector`, the API `POST /charge-token`) and what it calls (the API's `pay-connector/expect|result|card` and Sola), plus Sola's webhook to the API. The paths were read from `ConnectorApp` and the API's callers. | Owner decision 2026-11-23 (W3 brief, item 4). |
| W3-02 | 2026-11-23 | **W3 item 2: a web path for an account made by phone (V-05, V-09 follow-ups).** (1) **Sign-in by SMS code** (`PhoneSignInService`, `POST /auth/phone/start` and `/auth/phone/verify`): the phone line's limits, not a second set; **a code is texted ONLY to a number an eligible account holds** (orchestrator review of 21594b8: texting any number typed into the internet invites SMS pumping and toll fraud, costs money per message and texts people who never asked; the phone line's V-03 "always text" exists because there the caller holds the number). Nothing is enumerable: for any valid number the per-number, global and per-IP caps are counted, a code is drawn and hashed, a row is written (account id null when none) and the answer is the same `202 { challengeId, expiresInSeconds }`; only the provider call is skipped, and a FAILED send for an account also answers 202 (a 503 only an account could produce would tell; the row is marked used, no code works, the buyer asks again). Guessing on a challenge of an unknown number gets the same `401 invalid_code`; and the send never runs in the request (orchestrator review of f4363ad: waiting for the provider would make a real account answer slower): `PhoneSmsDispatcher` sends after the answer on its own scope (20 s timeout) and marks the row used when the send fails; the code lives only in that task's memory, never in a queue table (per instance: a process that stops in those seconds loses the text and the buyer asks again); six digits, 10 minutes, 5 tries (the fifth wrong one locks the code even for the right digits), single use, consumed in one UPDATE (parallel right answers make one session) after a try is counted in one UPDATE (parallel guesses never exceed five); the SMS caps are the phone line's own `AttemptLimiter` buckets (`voice-sms-number` per `Voice:MaxSmsPerNumberPerHour`, `voice-sms-all` per `Voice:MaxSmsPerHour`), shared, plus 10 starts per client address per hour; sent through the same `ISmsSender` (SignalWire). The phone line's `PhoneCodeService` itself is NOT reused: it keeps its challenge in a call's memory and lives under `Voice/**`, which worker V owns; its constants (digits, lifetime, tries) are. The code is an HMAC under its OWN key `Auth:PhoneCodeKey` (optional; unset turns the feature off, 503; when set: 32+ chars, never a dev default, different from every other key and from the Voice keys and the SignalWire secrets, checked at start-up), over purpose, challenge id and code. **The challenge row holds no phone number**: only the account id the number belonged to when the code was sent (null when none), the HMAC, tries and times (`phone_sign_in_challenges`, purged an hour after it ends, erased with the account; PII table). A failed send marks the row used (no live code after "we could not send it"). (2) **Who signs in this way:** an account whose number is PROVEN (`PhoneVerifiedE164`) and that has NO password, not deleted, staff-locked or demo-verified, checked again at verify time (a lock or a password set in between ends it). An account with a password signs in with it: a text message is never a second way past a password. A right code whose account stopped being eligible after it was sent (a password set, a lock, a deletion) answers `404 no_account`: only the holder of the number can see that. (3) **Sessions:** `AuthenticateAsync` accepted only accounts with a verified email, which made a phone account's web session impossible; it now also accepts an account with NO email at all and a proven phone (not demo-verified). An account with an UNVERIFIED email still gets no session (whoever typed an unproven address must not get in through it). (4) **Step-up** (O-11) for an account without a password: `POST /auth/reauthenticate/phone/start` texts the account's own proven number (the body names none, so nothing else can be texted) and `/auth/reauthenticate/phone` stamps the session; a step-up code works only for the session's own account, and sign-in and step-up codes never stand in for each other (purpose in the HMAC and the row). (5) **Add an email** (`POST /account/email`): V-13b's flow (pending address, mailed link, confirmed at the API's own page; an address another account holds is never attached and the answer is the same), for an account with no email, behind a recent sign-in (a verified email is a recovery path, so a borrowed session must not add one). The mail now says "on a phone call with Solvelt or on the Solvelt website". (6) **The first password** (`POST /account/password/set`): recent sign-in AND a verified email (the password signs in with the email, and a forgotten one is recovered through it); set once, in one conditional UPDATE (no password, same epoch), every session ends and a fresh token is returned (M1-87), and the usual "password changed" mail goes out. **Passkeys** use the existing registration (recent sign-in); the authenticator shows the account's name when it has no email. (7) **Forgot-password** for an account that never had a password: with no email it cannot be addressed at all, so any address answers the same silent `204` and nothing is mailed (the decoy path, M1-65); with a verified email and no password the reset link arrives and sets the first password, as for every account (it proves the mailbox). A PENDING address is not the account's and gets no reset link. Tested in `PhoneWebSignInTests`. **Not in this slice:** deleting an account made by phone on the web (the delete endpoint asks for a password; such an account deletes by phone or after setting a password). | Owner decision 2026-11-23 (W3 brief, item 2): a buyer whose account was made by phone must be able to use the website. |
| W3-01 | 2026-11-23 | **W3 item 1: services priced by quote are listed (implements proposal M6-11).** (1) **Mapping:** a `service` whose `service.pricingModel` is `quote` (any case) is READABLE: `HttpCatalogSource` maps it to a snapshot with `QuotePriced = true` and **no variants**, even when the document also carries an amount or variants (the pricing model wins, so a quote service is never booked or carted at a price the seller did not set for it); its currency is `price.currency` or, when absent, the marketplace's `USD`; `QuoteAvailable` is the listing's availability state (`available`, `in_stock`, ... as for variants). A PRODUCT is never quote-priced (no price anywhere stays `price`). The code `quote_price` is no longer produced; rows quarantined under it come back at their next fetch (the pending version is fetched again after its back-off) or at the next applied version. (2) **Read model:** `catalog_listings.IsQuotePriced` (bool, default false; `InitialCreate` regenerated with `tools/migrations/regenerate-initial-create.sh`, only that column differs). `PriceFromCents`/`PriceToCents` are kept at 0 and `CompareAtFromCents` null for such a row; they are never shown, sorted or filtered on. `Available` = `QuoteAvailable`. The sync refuses a quote-priced snapshot of a product or one with variants. A listing that changes from fixed to quote loses its variants (nothing stale can be carted). (3) **API:** listing cards, the detail and the wishlist card carry `priceMode`: `fixed` or `quote`; a `quote` listing's price fields are 0 and must not be shown. (4) **Sorting:** in `price_asc` AND `price_desc` every quote-priced listing comes after every priced one, ordered by id; the cursor key of a quote-priced row is `q` (a cursor in the tail continues by id only), so cursor walks and page numbers give the same order. (5) **Filters:** `priceMin`/`priceMax` (any value, also 0) leave quote-priced listings out; the price facet's range is over priced listings only (a result of only quote-priced listings has a null range, never "from $0"). Other facets (type, category, brand, seller, delivery, availability) count them as usual. (6) **Booking:** `POST /bookings/holds` for a quote-priced service answers `422 quote_required` ("This service needs a quote first.") **without calling the portal** and without a row, exactly the portal's own answer; the buyer asks through `POST /quotes` (unchanged, needs only a visible service). The cart already refuses services, and the wishlist watch sends no price-drop for a listing with no variant price. Voice is unaffected (its search asks for `type=product`). | Owner decision 2026-11-23 (W3 brief, item 1): the buyer side must work completely; quote services were invisible. |
| Q-01 | 2026-11-19 | **Q (API by V): quantity offers (portal #101, contract C18), ONE pricing function.** `Offers/OfferPricing.Price` is pure (no database, clock or I/O) and is the only place offer maths lives: the cart, the quote, the checkout, the order sent to the portal and the phone read-back all call it, so the web and the phone can never disagree. Rules are C18.1: one offer per line (most specific scope products > category > all, then the older offer), eligible units counted per offer (other sellers' and other brands' units never count), the highest tier reached prices EVERY eligible unit, `discountCents = max(0, unit - tier) x quantity` (a tier never raises a price, so a lower sale price stays), each earned gift once per order, gift lines at price, discount and tax 0. Money is `long` cents. **Tie-break (mirrors the portal exactly):** the portal orders equally specific offers by `Rank(scope)` (products, then category, then all; a category and its parent category have the SAME rank), then the offer's `createdAt` compared exactly, then the lower `offerId` as a lowercase hyphenated 36-character string compared ordinally (Yanky1-test-login-backend `src/Solvelt.Api/Offers/OfferIntake.cs:111`; the C18.2 feed item and `GET /catalog/offers/{id}` carry `createdAt`, ISO-8601 UTC, 7-digit precision, immutable: seller-backend a42c32b, orchestrator message). We project it as `catalog_offers.CreatedAtTicks` (a tick count: PostgreSQL timestamps keep microseconds only and the tie-break must be exact) and sort scope, `CreatedAt ?? FirstSeenAt`, `OfferId.ToString("D")` ordinal. **Now on the portal's main: seller-backend `a42c32b` sends `createdAt` on every offer feed item and on `GET /catalog/offers/{id}` (demo tombstones excepted), and its tie-break is exactly ours (scope rank, `createdAt`, offer id as the 36-character `"D"` string, ordinal).** We prefer `createdAt` everywhere; **rows projected before that (no `createdAt`) are backfilled**: each sync run fetches the document of up to 20 such rows, fills `CreatedAtTicks` without touching the version (a newer version that comes with it is applied normally), and a portal that still sends none is asked again only after a day (`CreatedAtCheckedAt`; a document read without `createdAt` is not re-asked at once). Test: a row first projected without `createdAt` gets it on a later sync and the tie-break then uses it instead of the first-seen order (red when the backfill is removed). **Fallback kept for rows that still have none** (an older portal, or a row not yet backfilled; the journey must pass before and after the portal's merge): our first-seen time, strictly increasing in feed order for offers first seen in one sync (`OfferSyncService`), never a shared timestamp that leaves the id to decide; wrong only when an older offer is first seen after a newer one. *Tests:* `OfferPricingTests` (15), `OfferSyncTests` (feed order keeps the older offer first; createdAt kept to the tick), tie-break tests (older createdAt wins with the higher id, one tick apart, equal createdAt falls to the lower id string, category vs parent category, fallback): 25 units at 90c = 2250, tiers 10 to 95c and 20 to 90c give 15 = 1425 and 25 = 2250, tier never raises, cross-seller and cross-brand excluded, one gift. | A second copy of the maths anywhere is how a phone total differs from the web total, or a charge differs from what the portal accepts. |
| Q-02 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: offer projection.** `catalog_offers` mirrors the portal's offer feed (`GET /catalog/offers`, `/offers/{id}`) with the SAME rules as listings: `OfferSyncService` (lease, cursor, pending fetch with backoff), events `offer.updated` / `offer.ended` handled by `OfferEventHandler` (stub row, never touches the network), version bookkeeping `Version` / `KnownVersion` / `UnlistedVersion`, tombstones with reason `ended`, `paused` or `deleted` (deleted has no version: gone for good, a stale page never brings it back), a stale page or event never lowers a version. Tiers, gifts and the scope are JSON text columns (read whole, never queried). **Demo offers are never visible.** An unreadable document is skipped and logged by id and version, never throws. The InitialCreate migration was regenerated by the script. *Tests:* `OfferSyncTests` (7). | The feed is the only source of truth; the cart must stop applying an offer the moment its tombstone is seen. |
| Q-03 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: which offers apply (`OfferLoader`).** Visible, inside `activeFrom`/`activeTo` NOW (checked in memory: SQLite and PostgreSQL order offset values differently and a seller has few offers), currency USD. The category scope uses the listing's category and every ancestor (`CategoryMap.PathAsync`). A gift is `included` unless the offer document says `available: false`, then `unavailable` and the order says so with `giftUnavailable: true`; it never rejects checkout (C18.1-7). | One loader, so cart, quote and voice read the same set. |
| Q-04 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: cart, quote, order shapes** (agreed with worker Q). Cart line: `discountCents` (0) and `offerId?`; `lineTotalCents` is NET of the discount; group: `discountCents` and `offers[{offerId, version, name, eligibleUnits, tier, nextTier, unitsToNextTier, discountCents, gifts[{productId, title, quantity, minQuantity, status: included/unavailable}]}]`; `subtotalCents` is the sum of the discounted lines. Quote: lines carry `discountCents`/`offerId`, groups carry `offers[{offerId, version, name, discountCents, giftUnavailable}]` and `gifts[{offerId, productId, title, quantity}]`; **tax is computed on the discounted amount, gift lines carry no tax**; the quote token (`QuoteSigner.Digest`) and the checkout fingerprint cover every discount, offer id, offer VERSION, gift and the giftUnavailable flag. Orders: `marketplace_order_lines` get `OfferId`, `DiscountCents`, `IsGift`, `GiftOfOfferId` (gift lines have no variant, price 0), `marketplace_order_groups` get `DiscountCents` and `OffersJson` (what is sent to the portal), with DB defaults for the new NOT NULL columns. Order intake sends `offers[{offerId, offerVersion, giftUnavailable?}]` and per line `offerId`, `discountCents` (price is the price BEFORE the discount: the portal recomputes) and the gift lines (`isGift`, `giftOfOfferId`, no `variantId`); shipping tax is spread over paying lines only. **Free-shipping and local-delivery thresholds use the DISCOUNTED subtotal (items minus discounts), matched to the portal, not guessed:** the portal's own local-delivery check computes `var subtotal = o.ItemsCents - o.DiscountCents` (Yanky1-test-login-backend `src/Solvelt.Api/Delivery/DeliveryService.cs:70`, then `ZoneEngine.Evaluate(..., subtotal)` in `Delivery/Zones.cs`), and the eligibility call the marketplace sends carries the discounted group subtotal. For the CARRIER free-shipping threshold the portal has no intake check (shipping is the marketplace's own computation from the profile, `docs/SHIPPING.md`: "free when the items subtotal reaches the threshold", which does not say whether discounts are subtracted): we keep the discounted subtotal, consistent with local delivery and the fee rule (`Fee = (items - discounts) x bps`, `docs/ORDERS.md`). **Confirmed by the portal** (seller-backend `479247d`, docs-only C18 clarification, SHIPPING section 4): the free-shipping threshold is compared with the DISCOUNTED subtotal, as local delivery and the fee already were. The tiered journey crosses the boundary on purpose (free over 23.00, discounted 22.50, list 25.00) so CI proves the portal accepts that order. The cart keeps its 20-per-line limit, so 25 units are several lines. | The portal checks every figure, so the order must carry the same ones the buyer saw. |
| Q-05 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: what happens when an offer changes (orchestrator ruling, accepted, with conditions).** The brief says "re-quote on offer_mismatch", but orders reach the portal from the paid-order outbox AFTER payment, and the portal accepts a version for 15 minutes after it stopped applying (C18 grace). The C18 checklist line ("on 422 offer_mismatch re-quote and ask the buyer to confirm") therefore cannot apply after payment: there is no buyer in the loop and charging a different amount would be a second charge. So: (1) **the pre-charge guard** is the existing quote binding: checkout re-prices with the shared function from the CURRENT projection and compares the signed token; a changed tier, gift or offer (including a tombstone) gives 409 `price_changed` with the fresh quote and `messageCode: offer_changed` (the token carries an `o` hint that the quote involved an offer; it is outside the digest and only picks the wording); nothing is charged. The phone path already reads the new total and asks again before any payment. (2) **A post-payment 422 `offer_mismatch`** (any kind, including `offer_missing`) marks the order failed with `failureCode offer_mismatch` and refunds in full through the same path as `invalid_order`; never a blind retry, never another charge; the portal's kinds and messages are logged (no PII); the buyer is mailed the reason. (3) **Risk:** if the projection lags the portal by more than the 15-minute grace, a buyer can be quoted a version the portal no longer accepts: the order is refunded, never mis-charged. Projection staleness is NOT yet monitored by a dedicated alert (the offer worker logs each run; a stale-feed alarm is a follow-up for the orchestrator). *Tests:* `OfferOrderTests` (changed tier, changed gift, tombstone: 409 and nothing charged; a 422 after payment: failed, full refund, no portal capture). | Money never moves for a figure the buyer did not see. |
| Q-06 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: voice.** `OrderReadBack.Build` reads the quote the web would show: "Your quantity discount saves you $X.", "You also get a free Card wallet.", "A free gift is unavailable right now."; the totals are the quote's own. The portal's own C18.6 wording on a stale offer BEFORE and AFTER payment (`479247d`) matches what the API does (409 `offer_changed` before paying, nothing charged; a post-payment `offer_mismatch` refunds in full), and it accepted that handling. | The phone total must equal the web total for the same cart (C18 checklist). |
| Q-07 | 2026-11-19 | **Q: listing badge and order view.** `GET /catalog/listings/{id}` has `offers` (empty or ONE offer: the one the shared pricing function says applies to this listing now, most specific wins, never stacked; tiers, gifts with `available`, `activeTo`), for "20+ for $0.90 each" and "free wallet from 10"; the card list is unchanged (the badge is on the product page). An existing test pinned the detail's property list; `offers` was added to it (a new field, nothing removed). The order view shows per line `discountCents`, `offerId`, `isGift`, and per group `discountCents` and `giftUnavailable` (read from the portal's intake answer, C18.4, and from our own flag). | The web needs both without a second request. |
| V-18 | 2026-11-19 | **V: a phone PIN (second factor for signing in by phone).** *Owner decision from Ronald, relayed by the buyer-marketplace orchestrator (the orchestrator offered (a) a keypad PIN or (b) a texted link for the password; Ronald's answer, verbatim):* “a and yes” *(“a” is this slice; “yes” was saved cards, V-16, V-17).* **Design (as the orchestrator specified):** the caller sets a numeric PIN on the KEYPAD (`<Gather input="dtmf">`, ended with pound), never spoken, never sent to the model, a text or a log; at least 6 and at most 12 digits; trivial PINs are refused (all the same digit, a straight ascending or descending run, a repeated pair such as 121212, a PIN that is part of the caller's own phone number); the server reads back the LENGTH only (“a PIN of seven digits”) and the caller types it again to confirm; three bad entries end the step. `set_my_pin` and `remove_my_pin` are for a caller verified by SMS code in this call AND need a FRESH code first (changing or resetting needs an SMS code, as the orchestrator required); removal is read back and needs the keypad yes. **Decision recorded (the orchestrator left it to me): the PIN is a SECOND FACTOR alongside the SMS code, not an alternative to it.** Caller ID alone never authenticates, and an SMS code alone is exposed to SIM swap and interception; an account WITHOUT a PIN keeps today's code-only sign-in. So for an account with a PIN the call is only a guest with a verified phone after the code, and becomes the account (cart moves, account tools work) only when the PIN is typed. **Storage and brute force:** `buyer_accounts.PinHash` (+ `PinFailures`, `PinLockedUntil`): the digits are HMAC-peppered with a key derived (HKDF) from `Voice:PhoneCodeKey` for this purpose and then hashed with the existing password hasher, so a database copy alone cannot be brute-forced offline (six digits are only a million guesses); every PIN try is RECORDED BEFORE it is compared, by one atomic UPDATE on the account that only succeeds while it has tries left, so parallel calls (from any numbers) can never get more than five guesses between them; the count is on the ACCOUNT and survives hang-ups and new calls; the fifth wrong try LOCKS PIN sign-in for every call until the holder unlocks it with a fresh SMS code and a NEW PIN (time does not unlock it; a caller whose PIN is locked is a guest with a verified phone, and `set_my_pin` then runs the unlock path); a right PIN, even as the fifth try, clears the count; a per-number ceiling (20 tries an hour) sits on top; the holder is TEXTED at the proven number when the PIN locks and when it is set, changed or removed; the PIN step can only be reached after the SMS code is right for that number and is opened only by the server (digits typed with no pending step get a hang-up; request logging records method, route and status only, never a body). **Residual risk, accepted by the orchestrator's design:** someone who holds the SIM can lock the PIN and then reset it with a fresh code, so against a SIM swap the PIN adds the lock-out, the new-PIN requirement and the text to the real holder, not a hard block. Phone only:** the web sign-in never looks at `PinHash` (test: the PIN is not a password). Erased at account deletion and redaction; never exported. No new secret (it reuses `Voice:PhoneCodeKey`). *Tests:* `VoicePinTests` (12), each red on revert (the PIN step itself, the record-before-compare gate with 14 parallel guesses, the lock texts, the change text, the unlock path, confirmation, the trivial-PIN rule). *Also here:* the one-line V-12 follow-up: the one OLD barge-in test that changed (`Barge_in...` in `VoiceCallTests`) was a correction of a test that encoded the bug, not a weakened test: its assertion value (2) is unchanged and the only edit is a 10 ms advance of the fake clock (the old test asserted bytes sent while the clock stood still). | A numeric PIN raises phone sign-in above "whoever holds the SIM" without any card or spoken secret reaching the model. |

| V-21 | 2026-11-19 | **V: Yiddish spelling in search: the digraph letters (slice `fix/v-yiddish-folds`; the website benefits too).** *Owner decision from Ronald, relayed by the buyer-marketplace orchestrator (his words, verbatim):* “Yes, go ahead with the Yiddish spelling fix in search (ײ = יי, װ = וו), on the website too.” **What:** `SearchText.Normalize` (the ONE tokenizer, used for the index text and for the query, so both sides fold) now writes the Yiddish digraph letters as the letter pairs they stand for: ײ (U+05F2) is יי, װ (U+05F0) is וו, ױ (U+05F1) is וי, and the pointed ligature ײַ (U+FB1F) is יי (it was the digraph letter ײ; the instruction was יי). Niqqud is still dropped, so ײַ, ייַ and יי are one word. **Rebuild:** the search rows hold text already normalised, so a change of the rules needs them rewritten. `SearchIndexer.ReindexStaleAsync` finds the rows written by the earlier rules by what only they leave behind, the raw digraph letters U+05F0 to U+05F2 in any of the four fields (`SearchText.StaleLetters`), normalises those fields again and rebuilds the typo-match text; a rewritten row no longer matches, so it is idempotent and a database with nothing stale does nothing. It works in **bounded batches** of 200 rows (each save is its own short transaction, so a large catalogue never holds one long transaction or blocks the sync poll) and handles each stale row once (a first version looped "until none are left", which a deliberate break showed could chase a row the rules cannot change forever). **A listing edited during the rewrite is not overwritten with older text:** each row is rewritten by one conditional UPDATE that applies only while the row still holds exactly the values that were read; a row the sync rewrote in the meantime already has the new rules' text and the rebuild yields (last write wins; a test plants that race, and breaking the condition turns it red). The catalog sync runs it once after an application start (a singleton `SearchRebuildGate`, so the scan is not repeated on every poll), before the poll, under the sync lease. Normalising the stored text again equals normalising the original, because the old rules left these letters in place (a fixed point). On PostgreSQL the `document` tsvector is a generated column of those fields, so it follows by itself and the `v3` column comment needs no bump; **no migration and no schema change.** *Decision I took (a first version recorded a tokenizer version in a second `catalog_sync_state` row; the existing sync tests rightly assume that table has one row, and I did not change them): detect stale rows by content instead of by a recorded version.* **Variants considered, NOT folded (documented as asked):** (1) final letter forms ך ם ן ף ץ to the regular ones: no false match would follow, but it changes Hebrew search on the website beyond the approved scope, and there is no demonstrated need; (2) unstressed ע, ײַ-versus-ײ and the other YIVO versus Hasidic differences: they are whole-word spelling differences (for example שבת/שאבעס), not letter folds, and folding letters for them would match different words; (3) a single yod/vov versus the pair: different words. Typo tolerance (pg_trgm and edit distance) already covers one-letter slips. *Tests:* `SearchTextYiddishTests` (each fold at the tokenizer, incl. the ligature, niqqud, the maqaf and other scripts unchanged), `CatalogSearchYiddishTests` (a listing typed with ײ found by יי and the reverse; the same for װ/וו; ײַ and ױ; unrelated words still do not match; the rebuild; Latin, Cyrillic and Hebrew still find what they found) and `VoiceYiddishSpellingTests` (the phone search): they run on the database the run uses, and were run on SQLite AND on PostgreSQL 16 here; the existing `CatalogSearch*` and `SearchText*` suites are unchanged and green on both (107 on PostgreSQL, none skipped). | A Yiddish title typed one way is found by a search typed the other way, by web and by phone, without changing Latin, Cyrillic or Hebrew search. |

| V-31 | 2026-11-20 | **V: a flaky test of the parallel engines, `Both_engines_answer_the_judge_chooses_and_exactly_one_text_leaves` (branch `fix/parallel-stt-race`).** *Seen:* it failed once on the main push of de2afb3 ("Assert.Single() failure: the collection was empty", 57 ms) and passed on the branch run and on later commits. *Cause:* the test emitted two finals and then asserted after a FIXED pause (50 ms of fake time plus about 20 ms of real time), but `ParallelStt` runs on the thread pool, so on a busy build agent the answer simply was not there yet: the test measured the speed of the machine. I found no race in `ParallelStt` itself (the engines' events go through buffered channels, every deadline is computed from the round's own start, nothing is dropped when two finals arrive together). *Proof:* delaying the coordinator by 150 ms in a scratch build made the old version of that test and seven siblings fail, among them exactly this one; with the fix the same slow build passes all 15 (the same delay, built, run three times). *Fix:* a final is now emitted through `Rig.Final`, which waits (real time) until the coordinator has TAKEN it before the fake clock may move (a small internal seam, `ParallelStt.IProbe.FinalsSeen`, a counter and nothing else); results are awaited with `WaitForFinals` (real-time polling, and the fake clock creeps on while nothing has arrived, because a timer created after the test stopped moving time would otherwise never fire), then a short settle so a wrongly doubled final still shows. No retry, no skip, no looser assertion: every assertion of the class is unchanged in what it checks. | The parallel-engine tests no longer depend on how fast the agent is. **Second occurrence and the real cause (branch `fix/v-judge-budget-flake`, 2026-11-22; CI run 45043053967 on dc5bc58, a menu-wording-only commit, failed `The_budget_counts_from_the_first_final_so_waiting_for_a_slow_engine_shortens_the_judges_time`: expected "fast enough", got "alpha").** The first hypothesis (a judge entering after the budget timer) is wrong: the judge and the budget timer are created in the same synchronous run of `DecideAsync`. The race is earlier. `Final(B)` returns as soon as the coordinator has TAKEN the final, but the coordinator reads the clock for `spent` a little after that; if the test's fake clock moves 100 ms or more in that gap (10 `Pump` steps, easy on a loaded runner) the budget left is shorter than the judge's 700 ms and the fallback "alpha" wins. Proof by fault injection: a 300 ms sleep between taking the final and reading the clock made the test fail with exactly the CI message before the fix and pass after it. Fix (test seam only, product behaviour unchanged): `ParallelStt.IProbe.DecisionsArmed` counts rounds that have asked their judges and armed the budget wait (after `spent`); the rig's `Armed()` waits for it in real time (10 s, asserted) after the final that completes a round and BEFORE the clock moves, in the judge tests that pump after a completed round (the 400/700/900/1200 ms test, the slow judge, the stubborn judge). Same assertions, same numbers, nothing skipped or loosened. |

| V-38 | 2026-11-22 | **V: the stream codec stays PCMU; no `Voice:StreamCodec`, no L16 (orchestrator's decision after reading SignalWire's docs; branch `feat/v-asr-codec`).** The brief asked for a configurable stream codec (PCMU@8000h default, L16@16000h, L16@24000h) with L16 handling in the bridges. SignalWire's call-streaming page (https://signalwire.com/docs/platform/voice/call-streaming, "Choose a stream codec for the call") says: "A stream can't carry more detail than the call does. Streaming a call that uses an 8 kHz codec at 16 kHz only resamples the same audio." and "Calls to and from phone numbers use a codec SignalWire sets, and the `codecs` settings in SWML have no effect on them. Stream them with the default `PCMU`." All our calls are phone-number calls, so L16 would add NO audio quality, only move the resampling to SignalWire. It would also add a failure mode: "SignalWire doesn't start a stream whose codec it doesn't accept" (the `codec_error` status is "Requested codec not enabled for your account, or multi-channel audio requested"; how an account enables a codec is not stated), and the stream does not fall back to PCMU. On a stream with another codec the audio WE send back must be in that codec too ("on a bidirectional stream converts the audio your server sends back into the call's codec"; the cXML Stream page, https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice/stream, lists the payload encodings), so every vendor path would need converting; `L16` is little-endian, frames are 20 ms (160 bytes PCMU, 640 bytes L16 at 16 kHz). Every recogniser we use already takes 8 kHz mu-law natively (Deepgram mulaw/8000, Gladia wav/ulaw 8000, OpenAI audio/pcmu, Yiddish Labs through our WAV wrap). **So: nothing is built** (no setting, no `codec` attribute, no L16 paths); option 6's Gather is unaffected (it is not a stream). **One small guard:** when the start event's `start.mediaFormat` is present and is not `audio/x-mulaw` at 8000 Hz (SignalWire reports `audio/x-mulaw` or `audio/x-L16`, 8000, 16000 or 24000, one channel), both bridges log ONE warning per stream (the encoding label only when it is short and plain text, and the rate) and carry on; a start event without a media format claims nothing and logs nothing. Tests: `VoiceStreamFormatTests`; a mutation of the once-only guard fails one. | L16 costs a failure mode and a conversion layer in every vendor path for no gain on a phone call. The guard turns a surprising format into one clear log line instead of a call that sounds wrong for no visible reason. |

| V-44 | 2026-11-23 | **Fix: a request could answer 500 on SQLite when a connection could not be opened for a moment (branch `fix/reviews-parallel-writers`; the orchestrator's brief after `ReviewsTests.The_figures_of_the_listing_the_seller_and_the_brand_follow_creation_edits_and_parallel_writers` failed once on main's SQLite job, run 45080952872, 4 of 6 parallel writes got 500).** ROOT CAUSE, from a local reproduction (that test looped under CPU load, 1 failure in 86 runs, 4 of 6 writers failing in it): `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.SqliteException`, SQLITE_BUSY (5) "database is locked" or "not an error", thrown from `SqliteConnection.Open()` (auth read, review create) and from the BEGIN of a transaction. It is the SQLite provider (development and test), not the review logic, and PostgreSQL passed. It is NOT ordinary write contention: the database is in WAL mode and a side connection holding BEGIN IMMEDIATE or EXCLUSIVE for 1.5 s made the request wait and succeed. It looks like an open against a connection that is closing or checkpointing the log, which SQLite refuses at once; that last step is NOT PROVEN (it could not be made deterministic, a 12-writer stress test failed 0 of 32). Only the address book and bookings retried a busy SQLite and answered 503; every other path had no retry and answered 500. FIX, SQLite only: (1) `SqliteBusyRetry.Opening` and `.Beginning` interceptors open the connection and begin the transaction themselves, retried on SQLITE_BUSY or LOCKED only, 4 attempts, pauses of 15, 30 and 60 ms (105 ms in all, far under a second); a constraint or any other failure is not retried; (2) what still fails is answered by the exception middleware as the existing 503 `too_busy` with Retry-After 1 (SqliteException 5 or 6 only, directly or wrapped), never a 500. The interceptors are registered only for SQLite in `SqliteBusyRetry.Configure`, the one place the provider is chosen; a test asserts PostgreSQL has none. The reviews test is unchanged. PROOF: deterministic tests (`SqliteBusyRetryTests`, 12): a forced busy then success, a busy that never clears escapes after exactly 4 attempts in under a second, constraint codes are not retried, the interceptor opens through the retries, an escaped busy error is 503 with Retry-After and 500 for anything else (mutation: disabling the middleware branch fails the 503 tests); the rate itself cannot be proven at about 1 in 100 runs, so the looped run after the fix is evidence, not proof. Open: the real cause of the refused open is not identified inside SQLite or the driver; if it recurs after this, the next step is a log of the open attempts. | Ronald, through the orchestrator (the failure and the root-cause request) |

The voice (speech output) and the text model are not used, so their settings need not be real; option 5's judge, if switched on, needs its real key. NOT REPLAYABLE, said in the report: option 1 (OpenAI's speech-to-speech line) and option 6 (SignalWire's own recogniser inside a call) take no recorded audio; the OpenAI transcription recogniser of slice B will be replayable the moment it exists as an `IStreamingStt`. NUMBERS: word error rate and character error rate over CORPUS totals (all errors over all reference words, not the mean of per-phrase rates, so a long phrase counts for more), substitutions, deletions and insertions apart, per language and per kind (plain, shop words, digits, address), the median delay from the end of the audio to the last final words, a per-recording table (said and heard), and a CSV with spreadsheet formulas neutralised. NORMALISATION, the same on both sides: case, punctuation, Hebrew points, geresh, the Yiddish digraph letters (as in search, V-21), final letter forms and the way a digit is written ("4" or "four") are not errors; a different word is. RECORDING BY PHONE (built on the orchestrator's SignalWire facts, read from the docs on 2026-11-23: cXML Record, the recordings fetch and delete calls): with `Voice:TestSet:Enabled` the test-line menu (test numbers only) adds the line "To record the test set, say nine." and nine starts the flow (it needs no menu wording of its own, so it holds whichever way the menu is spoken). Each phrase is read (an English one by the voice; a Yiddish one is "read it from your list": the voice cannot read Yiddish), then Record with `playBeep` true, `trim` trim-silence, `timeout` 3, `maxLength` from `Voice:TestSet:MaxRecordSeconds`, `transcribe` false (an extra charge), no `finishOnKey`, and a `recordingStatusCallback` for `completed`; then a SPEECH Gather: next, again or stop (an unclear answer is asked once more, then the call ends; silence three times ends it; nine takes of a phrase is the limit). The phrase, take and an anonymous speaker label (a keyed hash of the number, never the number) travel in the signed URL, so nothing is kept on the server between steps; the speaking steps are for the menu's test numbers only. The CALLBACK only queues an outbox job (dedupe key = the recording sid, so a retried callback is one job); the outbox worker does the work with retries: fetch the WAV (Basic authentication with the SignalWire project id and token; a 404 until it is ready is a retry), check it is audio, store it in the container under a name made of the phrase, the speaker label, a short code of the call and the take (so two sessions never meet in one name), CHECK THE STORED SIZE, and only then DELETE the recording at SignalWire (already gone counts as deleted). A failed delete is retried and does not store a second copy; a copy that cannot be verified is never followed by a delete; a different recording already under the name, or something that is not audio, ends the job as dead with the recording LEFT at SignalWire (audio that was not stored is never deleted), and the error log names the sid. The `storageUrl` attribute (straight to Azure) is not used: it would need a SAS URL as a secret. The SignalWire settings are the existing `SignalWire:Space`, `SignalWire:ProjectId` and `SignalWire:ApiToken` (the same ones that send the text messages), and the token must carry the Management scope (to fetch, with Protect Recording Media URLs on) and the Voice scope (to delete): the start-up check asks for them when the test set is on. The status callback method is set explicitly, `recordingStatusCallbackMethod="POST"` (the Record page lists it: "default: POST. The type of HTTP request to use when requesting a recordingStatusCallback", per the orchestrator's correction), so the endpoint and the request always agree. A speaker may also record elsewhere and use `upload`. COST AND PRIVACY: a run sends the recorded voices to the vendors in the matrix, so the speakers must agree to that (the owner and staff who volunteer), a speaker label is a code or first name, and the container is the only copy kept (the tool reads audio into memory and drops it). NOT TESTED against any real vendor or storage account (no keys here): tests use a fake recogniser and a memory store; the first real run is the owner's. | Ronald, through the orchestrator |

| V-53 | 2026-11-24 | **Fix: a helpful vote could answer 503 too_busy when SQLite refused a lock in the middle of its transaction (branch `fix/v-vote-busy`; follow-up of V-44; found by main CI run 45165542168 on 701945f, SQLite job only: `ReviewsTests.A_helpful_vote_is_one_per_buyer_even_in_parallel...`, 1 of 6 parallel votes answered `{"code":"too_busy"}`).** WHAT I KNOW: the failing line, the answer, and that the vote path (`ReviewService.VoteAsync`) begins a transaction, inserts the vote (INSERT OR IGNORE) and adds to the counter. WHAT I INFER (not observed: 17 stressed local runs of the test, with 6 CPU burners on 4 cores, did not reproduce it): the API's SQLite runs in the default rollback-journal mode, where a connection holding a read lock that asks for the write lock while another writer is waiting for that read lock to clear is refused AT ONCE with SQLITE_BUSY (the busy timeout is not used, because waiting would deadlock); six parallel writers to the same database can do exactly that part-way through a transaction. WHY V-44'S 105 ms WAS NOT ENOUGH HERE: V-44 retries only OPENING the connection and BEGINNING the transaction, where the refusal came from a contended open; this refusal comes after the transaction has begun, from a lock already held by another writer, and a retry of one statement cannot help because the transaction itself must give up its read lock and start again. FIX: `SqliteBusyRetry.RunTransactionAsync` runs a whole short transaction again (5 attempts in all, pauses 20, 40, 80 and 160 ms, 300 ms at most) when SQLite answers BUSY or LOCKED; a refused attempt is rolled back by its disposal; the vote path uses it (its insert is idempotent by the unique key, so a repeat never double counts). What still fails after the attempts is the same exception and the same 503 too_busy as before. POSTGRESQL does not change: it never raises a SQLite exception, so its work runs exactly once, as before. The assertions of the existing test are untouched (all 6 parallel votes still 204, one vote per buyer). PROOF BY FAULT INJECTION: `VoteBusyFault` (an EF command interceptor in `ReviewsTests`) refuses the first two inserts of a vote with SQLITE_BUSY in the middle of the transaction; the new test `A_helpful_vote_refused_with_busy_in_the_middle_of_its_transaction_is_run_again_and_answers_204_with_one_vote_counted` BEFORE the fix: FAILS (expected NoContent, actual ServiceUnavailable, the symptom of the CI run); AFTER the fix: passes (204, three attempts, one vote counted once), and a lock that never clears still ends as the bounded 503 with nothing half-written. 129 related tests pass. NOT DONE: the other writers that begin their own transactions (carts, addresses, checkout) have V-44 only; if the same refusal shows in CI for one of them, `RunTransactionAsync` is the tool to wrap it (I did not guess). | Orchestrator (CI finding, 2026-11-24) |

| V-57 | 2026-11-25 | **Fix: words the caller said could be dropped from a turn, because the silence turn detector was called from two threads with no lock (branch `fix/v-turn-detector-threads`; the orchestrator's brief after the `VoiceNBestTests` failure in run 618).** `SilenceTurnDetector` keeps the finals of the turn in a plain list. `OnEvent` (the recogniser reader) adds to it and `OnTick` (the 50 ms timer) joins and then clears it, on a different thread, so a final added between the join and the clear was in no turn, and a concurrent add and read of a `List` is not safe at all. `VoiceTurnDetectorConcurrencyTests` runs a reader of 200,000 finals against a ticker: before the change 177,628 of 200,000 came out (22,372 lost); after it, all of them, each once. This is a real bug, but it is NOT shown to be the cause of the `VoiceNBestTests` failure (there the bridge adds to its own `_turnFinals` before the detector sees the final, so the note's finals were present); that test's cause is still open and V-56 makes its next failure say why. | Orchestrator (brief, 2026-11-25) |

`To` and `From` E.164, `Url`, `Method`, `StatusCallback`, `StatusCallbackMethod`, `Timeout` (default 60), `MachineDetection` (Enable, DetectMessageEnd, none), `Record`; answer: `sid`, `status`, `direction` (`outbound-api`), `answered_by` (human or machine); errors `code`, `message`, `more_info` (only the HTTP status and `code` are logged); our cXML fetch carries `CallSid`, `CallStatus`, `Direction` (`outbound-api`), `From` (our number), `To` (the customer); source https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/rest/calls/create-a-call and https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice. **Product decisions (orchestrator):** From = `SignalWire:FromNumber`; no calling-hours window (30-minute expiry bounds it); MachineDetection=Enable; a machine or a person who is not confirmed hears only the neutral line; before `<Pay>` a human confirms with a spoken yes; no retry; staff view = `voice.view`, read only, masked, no reveal; Timeout 30. **My decisions:** (1) the confirmation question carries NO order detail (the orchestrator's example named the last digits of the order; a wrong person would hear them), the amount is read only after the yes: tell me if you want the digits back. (2) `StatusCallbackEvent` is not sent: its default (completed) is the wanted event, the answer to the fetch itself marks the call answered, and how several values are written is not on the page (unverified, so not guessed). (3) Because the page on signatures of outbound fetches could not be read, every webhook needs SignalWire's signature AND our own signed token (HMAC under its own label, bound to the callback and the 30 minutes) AND the right state; the start-up is one-time because the row moves `dialing` to `answered` exactly once; direction must be `outbound-api`, `To` must equal the callback's number, the sid must be the one placed. (4) The buyer's order endpoints show only placed and paid orders, so an unpaid order is found by the server: an account's own pending order, or for a caller without an account an order whose payment was tried by phone from the same verified number; with no number given the newest is meant. (5) One active callback per order by a filtered unique index, three a day per number, 45 seconds before the dial so the caller's call can end; callbacks past their time expire (and free the order). (6) The keypad payment step of a callback call ends the call (no assistant to go back to); the callback is `paid` only when the order is paid. (7) Audit: every state change is an event row with a fixed word; the order number is logged, the number never. (8) `AnsweredBy` is read when present; whether SignalWire sends it with the fetch is not on the pages read, so a machine that does not announce itself is caught by the missing yes. (9) **The edge of the 30 minutes (the orchestrator's review):** the token now lives to `ExpiresAt` plus a grace (`Voice:PayCallback:GraceMinutes`, 20) and is used by the confirm, keypad and status steps of a call that was answered inside the window; a call is not placed when less than `MinMinutesLeftToDial` (5) minutes of the window are left (the callback expires, event `too_late_to_dial`); the sweep ends a not-yet-answered callback at `ExpiresAt`, but leaves an answered or paying one alone until the grace has passed and then records `paid` when the ORDER is paid, otherwise `expired`; an answer after `ExpiresAt` still starts nothing. Before this a payment running past minute 30 could be marked expired mid-payment and the end-of-call webhook got a 403, so the record said expired for a paid order (the money was never affected). Tests: a dial skipped near expiry, a payment finishing at minute 33 recorded as paid, the status word accepted inside the grace and refused past it, the sweep settling by the order. **UNVERIFIED and listed for the first real call (VOICE.md section 19, step 13):** `<Pay>` on an outbound call; the signature on outbound fetches; concurrency and calling-hours limits. Nothing was run against SignalWire: all tests use fakes (17 tests, `VoicePayCallbackTests`). Docs: `docs/VOICE.md` section 22. | Orchestrator (brief, 2026-11-25) |
| V-17 | 2026-11-19 | **V: paying with a saved card by phone.** *Follows the owner's “a and yes” (V-16) and the orchestrator's order of slices.* **Flow:** `pay_for_order` with `method=saved_card` and the `position` of a card in the list `list_saved_cards` just read out (verified ACCOUNT only; the order must already be read back and confirmed). The model chooses nothing but that position; the SERVER reads back the card and the amount (“I will charge your Visa card ending 1 1 1 1, expiring 7 twenty 31, $35.00 for your order”) and only the caller's yes or 1 on the keypad step `confirm_card_pay` charges it; no or silence charges nothing and places nothing. On yes the order is placed by the same path as every payment (priced by the server, same limits), a `saved_card` attempt is opened for exactly that total, the token is **decrypted only at that moment** (`SavedCardVault`, account-bound; a token that does not decrypt charges nothing and is logged for a person) and sent only to the connector's signed `/charge-token` (HMAC-SHA256 of `{ts}.{body}`, five-minute window, the answer signed too). The connector asks THIS API what to charge for the attempt (`expect`: amount and invoice from the order, purpose `sale`), runs one `cc:sale` with `xToken`, and reports its result over the usual signed `result` channel, which settles through the SAME amount-and-invoice check (`SettleAsync`) as every payment and sends the confirmation text; the API then reads the settled attempt to decide what the caller hears (paid, being confirmed, or did not go through). **Not found for another account:** the card is looked up by account id (the call's, never a model-supplied id); another account's card id is simply not found and charges nothing. **Idempotent, unknown never retried:** the connector charges once per attempt (a repeat replays the stored answer); an answer that is not clear (connector unreachable, not authentic, Sola unknown) leaves the attempt `pending`, is logged at error level for a person, and a second saved-card try for the same order while that attempt is pending is answered “being confirmed” and charges nothing; a declined sale leaves the order for another try, which is a new attempt. No security code is asked (V-16). Sandbox only; real money stays off. *Tests:* `VoiceSavedCardPayTests` (8, the real connector handler and a fake Sola behind the real API test server), each red on revert (account scoping of the card, the pending guard, charging only on yes). | The owner wants saved cards usable by phone; the connector keeps the token and the card path out of the API's reach except for one decrypted-for-one-sale call. |
| V-16 | 2026-11-18 | **V: saved cards by phone, through the pay connector (the saving half; paying with one is the next slice).** *Owner decision from Ronald, relayed by the buyer-marketplace orchestrator (typed by Ronald in its chat; the orchestrator asked whether to save payment cards through the keypad connector), his answer verbatim:* “a and yes” *(the “yes” is for saved cards; “a” was the phone PIN, a later slice).* **Docs checked first (primary sources):** SignalWire `<Pay chargeAmount="0" tokenType="reusable">` sends the connector `chargeAmount "0"` and `token_type "reusable"` and wants `{token_id, error_code, error_message}`; Sola `cc:save` (xCardNum, xExp, optional xCVV/xZip) returns `xToken`, `xMaskedCardNumber`, `xCardType`, `xExp` and charges nothing; a later `cc:sale` takes `xToken` instead of the card. **Built:** `add_saved_card` (keypad step `save_card`: the model is off the line, the `<Pay>` instruction goes to our connector, which asks Sola for a token and replies `token_id`), `list_saved_cards` (brand, last four, expiry; never the token), `remove_saved_card` (server read-back + keypad yes, like every account change). Only a caller verified by SMS code in THIS call, with an account, can do any of it; at most 5 cards per account, 2 save tries per call. **What is stored:** new table `saved_cards` (account, Sola token, brand, last four, expiry MMYY, created) and nothing else: never a card number, never a security code (this API has never seen one). The connector reports `{attemptId, status, token, brand, last4, exp}` to the API over the SAME signed channel (`POST /api/v1/voice/pay-connector/card`; the API does not take a credential from SignalWire's action: `PayResult` only decides what the caller hears, the stored attempt decides what happened); the attempt is a `voice_payment_attempts` row of method `save_card`, amount 0, whose order field carries `cs-{accountId}` so the report knows whose card it is. `expect` tells the connector `purpose: save_card` (amount 0) or `sale`; the connector refuses a save unless the SERVER opened a save attempt and the request says amount 0, and refuses a sale whose purpose is not `sale`. Idempotent on `transaction_id`; an unclear save is never retried. **Token protection (orchestrator review):** the token is a payment credential for our own Sola account, so it is **encrypted at rest** (`SavedCardVault`: AES-256-GCM, a key derived with HKDF from `Sola:SavedCardKey` for this purpose alone, a fresh nonce per value, the ACCOUNT ID as associated data, so a row copied to another account or edited does not decrypt; the column holds only ciphertext, up to 400 characters; the duplicate guard is a keyed HMAC-SHA256 fingerprint of the token in `TokenHash`, unique per account, since a unique index over ciphertext with a random nonce would guard nothing). It is never logged, never exported beyond brand/last four/expiry, and never given to the model (it sees position, brand, last four and expiry). **Bound to the account** that created it (the removal tool and its apply step only ever see the caller's own ids; a later sale will only decrypt with the account's own id). **The tokenise report is bound to an open save attempt this server created for this call and account** (signed; the attempt names the account; while the call is live its signed-in account must match, else `wrong_account` and nothing is stored; a replay changes nothing). `Sola:SavedCardKey` (>= 32 characters, differs from every other secret) is required with the connector; losing it makes saved cards unreadable (they are saved again). **Connector ledger:** the token is wiped (set to NULL) the moment the API acknowledges it, and a token the API has not taken within `Connector:SavedTokenMinutes` (default 30) is wiped by the background sweeper, the row becomes `abandoned` (`token_not_delivered`), is logged at error level for a person and reported to the API as an unclear save.
**Connector ledger:** a `save` row keeps the token, brand, last four and expiry ONLY until the API has them (see above), so the ledger file can briefly hold a Sola token: treat the file as a secret (VOICE.md). **Erasure and export:** saved cards are deleted at once with the account (deletion and redaction), listed in the PII table, and exported without the token. **Decision (CVV, recorded as asked): NO security-code re-entry for a later sale with a saved card.** SignalWire `<Pay>` cannot collect a security code alone, so a re-prompt would need a `<Gather>` whose digits reach this API, SignalWire's webhook and our logs, putting sensitive authentication data into the API's scope; the protections instead are the SMS-verified account in THIS call, the card chosen by last four, the server read-back and yes, per-call limits, and amount and invoice from the server. Caveat: Sola says `cc:sale` "may require xCVV depending on the merchant's security settings": if the sandbox merchant demands it, saved-card sales stay blocked until that setting is relaxed (to be seen at the first sandbox call), and nothing will work around it. **Built but not yet used:** the connector's signed `/charge-token` endpoint (a `cc:sale` with `xToken` for an attempt the API opened, amount and invoice from the API's own expectation, one charge per attempt, unknown never retried) and its tests; the API does not call it yet: paying with a saved card (pick by last four, read-back, yes) is the next slice. Sandbox only; real money stays off. *Tests:* `VoiceSavedCardTests` (10) and `SavedCardTests` in the connector project (12), each red on revert (account scoping of removal, the cap, the shape check, erasure at deletion). | Saved cards were asked for by the owner; keeping the card path inside the connector keeps the API out of card-data scope. |

Both are signed in both directions (`X-Connector-Timestamp` + `X-Connector-Signature` = base64 HMAC-SHA256 of `{unix ts}.{body}`, five-minute window), and the report settles through the same `PhoneOrderService.SettleAsync` as every other provider (amount must equal the order total, invoice must be our order number; a repeat changes nothing). **Hardening:** SignalWire's `X-SignalWire-Signature` (HMAC-SHA1 of the URL without credentials plus the raw body, project Signing Key) AND Basic auth, both checked before the body is read as a card; no IP allowlist (SignalWire's connector requests come from no published range); TLS only (plain http refused unless `Connector:RequireHttps=false` on a development box; `TrustForwardedProto` for a TLS-terminating platform); card number and security code held only in pooled byte arrays that are zeroed on dispose (no string fields for them; the request buffer to Sola is zeroed too); no card data in logs, exceptions or metrics (a log-fuzz test feeds PAN-like input through every failure path and scans every log line, exception text included); the amount is the server's (a request whose `chargeAmount` differs from it, even in format, charges nothing); idempotent on SignalWire's `transaction_id` (a repeat replays the stored answer, never a second sale; concurrent repeats charge once); per-attempt request limit (`Connector:MaxRequestsPerAttempt`, default 3) and a per-charge cap (`MaxChargeCents`, default $500); a paid attempt takes no further request; **an unknown Sola outcome is never retried** (the card may have been charged: one call, logged at error level for a person, the attempt takes no new charge); anything that fails after the sale (the ledger, a disposal, a bug, the sale call itself throwing) leaves the row `unknown` with the reference, logged at error level with attempt, transaction and reference only, and is answered `gateway_unknown`; a row still `processing` after `SolaTimeoutSeconds` + 60 s (its request died) is swept to `unknown` by the retry service and reported for a person (orchestrator review of ab10dbf); a failure only in REPORTING a result that was already recorded leaves the row as it is (an approved sale stays approved, unreported, delivered later by the retry service) and the caller hears the real answer (orchestrator review of b02ae28); an approval for another amount than asked is not told to SignalWire as paid and is reported as it was; results the API cannot take yet are kept in the ledger and re-reported by a background service. **Sandbox only:** the connector refuses to start in Production; real money stays off (see V-07 and the parked Stripe-versus-Sola decision). **Open and unproven:** nothing has run against live SignalWire or Sola yet: the URL normalisation behind the signature (default port, trailing slash) and SignalWire's `X-SignalWire-SHA256` header are verified only against the documentation; | V-12 | 2026-11-18 | **Fix: barge-in on a real call ("it keeps talking even when I talk, and only responds after it's done", Ronald, test site).** *Cause (diagnosis of the portal orchestrator, verified in `RealtimeBridge.cs` of main cb9a402):* `_playing` tracked GENERATION, not PHONE PLAYBACK. It was set on each audio delta and cleared on `response.done`; OpenAI generates audio much faster than real time, so `response.done` arrives while the phone still has seconds of audio queued; `speech_started` then saw not-playing, sent no `clear`, and `interrupt_response` did nothing because the response was already done, so the new answer queued behind the old audio. The truncate used bytes SENT, not bytes PLAYED. The old test passed only because its fake model sent `speech_started` before `response.done`. *Fix (`PhonePlayback`, new):* after EVERY audio message the bridge sends a mark with a unique name (`a1`, `a2`, ...); SignalWire echoes a mark when the audio before it has played, or immediately if nothing is queued, and returns the marks of cleared audio after a `clear` (verified in SignalWire's stream documentation, https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice/stream.md, sections 'Send a mark message' and 'Send a clear message'); "the phone is playing" means some audio mark has not come back. The hand-off mark `end` (keypad steps, goodbyes) is a separate name and works as before. *What the caller heard:* played ms = min(wall time since playback began, audio sent), per item (playback begins with the first audio sent while the phone queue was empty; an item queued behind another starts after what is ahead of it), measured on the injected `TimeProvider` so tests control it. *On `speech_started` with audio queued on the phone,* whether or not `response.done` has arrived: `clear` to the phone, `conversation.item.truncate` of the last item at the played ms, `response.cancel` if the response is still active, one information-level log line (call id, queued ms, played ms; no content), and the playback state resets at once (the cleared marks that echo later are ignored). With nothing queued: no clear, no truncate. A lost echo cannot wedge it: past the end of everything sent plus a 3 s grace the audio counts as played. *Tests:* `VoiceBargeInTests` (8: regression after `response.done` with the played time, not the sent time; response still active also cancels; nothing queued means no clear and no truncate; a clear resets and the next response plays normally; a second interruption before the echoes finds nothing; lost echo; keypad end mark with audio marks in flight; the log line) and the old barge-in test (now lets 10 ms of playback pass, so its 2 ms is `min(played, sent)`; its assertion value is unchanged); 12 single-line reverts each turned a test red. *Test infrastructure:* the scripted hand-off now waits for the mark NAMED `end` (audio marks precede it in a real call). *Not proven:* nothing here ran against a real SignalWire call; it follows their documented mark/clear semantics, and the real test is a call on the test site with the interruption lines (VOICE.md section 9, item 3). | Ronald's real call on the test site, relayed by the orchestrator 2026-11-18. |

For an off-topic request: no detail and no partial answer, call `off_topic`, say ONLY the sentence it returns, go back to where things stood. An interrupted order follows today's rule: none exists before the payment step. *Decisions of mine:* (1) a MIXED request (an on-topic question with an off-topic tail) gets the on-topic part answered and the tail redirected, and the tail COUNTS as a strike (strict and simple; the orchestrator suggested it); (2) the redirect text comes from the server, not from the model; (3) the strike is not 'the model's judgement of intent': it is whatever the model reports with `off_topic`, so a model that never calls it is not limited by layer 2. *Layer 3 (a server-side transcript watchdog) was considered and deferred by Ronald.* *Known limit:* the rule is enforced by the model's instructions plus a server strike limit; a determined caller may get a sentence off topic before the limit. *Tests:* `VoiceTopicTests` (11) and the range check in `VoiceUnitTests`; each line was reverted in turn and its test went red (15 reverts, listed in the PR). | Brief V2 (docs/agent-briefs/V2-voice-topic-limit.md), Ronald's requirement relayed by the orchestrator 2026-11-18. |

| V-03 | 2026-11-17 | **Slice 2: caller identification, catalog and cart tools.** *Identification:* the caller's number comes from SignalWire's `From` (E.164, kept only in the call's memory state; the database keeps the masked form) and caller ID alone authenticates nobody. The tool `identify_caller` ALWAYS texts a six-digit code to that number (or to a number the caller names), whether or not an account uses it, so the answer cannot be used to find out who is a customer; it then puts the call on the keypad step. The code: `RandomNumberGenerator`, six digits, HMAC-SHA256 with its OWN key `Voice:PhoneCodeKey` (start-up refuses it equal to the stream token key or a SignalWire secret) over `call id | number | code` (a code is useless for another call or number), kept only as a hash in the call state, valid 10 minutes, 5 tries (the 5th wrong one locks it, even for the right code afterwards), consumed on success. Sends: at most 3 per call, 3 per number per hour and `Voice:MaxSmsPerHour` (300) in total (`AttemptLimiter`, per instance); a failed send leaves no challenge. The SMS is sent by SignalWire (`POST https://{Space}.signalwire.com/api/messaging/messages`, JSON `to`/`from`/`body`, basic auth project id and API token; verified at https://signalwire.com/docs/apis/rest/messages/create-message.md on 2026-11-17; production start-up needs `SignalWire:ProjectId`, `ApiToken`, `Space`, `FromNumber`); development and tests use `InMemorySmsSender`. Nothing logs a body, a number or a code (failures log a status code only). *Keypad:* when the model ends the stream for the keypad (`EndCall` after its goodbye, `KeypadPause` decided before either socket closes, because SignalWire's next instruction can arrive the moment the socket closes), `after-stream` answers `<Gather input="dtmf" numDigits="6" timeout="20" finishOnKey="#" actionOnEmptyResult="true" action=".../keypad">` with the platform's own `<Say>` (attributes verified at https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice/gather.md); `POST /api/v1/voice/signalwire/keypad` (signed; the signature covers the digits) checks them against the call's hash and answers another Gather (wrong), or `<Say>` plus a fresh stream (right, or locked/expired: then the call goes on, the summary says the caller is NOT verified and the agent can offer a person). No digit reaches OpenAI or a log; the model sees only the outcome in the server's summary. *Who the caller becomes:* a right code proves the caller holds the number. (Superseded by V-05: only a PROVEN phone counts.) If EXACTLY ONE buyer account (not deleted, not staff-locked, email verified) has that number as its profile phone (free text the buyer typed, compared in E.164 after narrowing by the last four digits), the caller is that account for the rest of the call (level verified, verified in this call) and a guest cart is merged into the account's cart with the existing `MergeGuestAsync`; with none, or more than one, the caller is a guest with a verified phone. The profile phone is not itself verified, so recognition always needs the code, and two accounts claiming one number are never guessed between. *Tokens:* each stream token now carries a random nonce and is single use (replay of a URL or upgrade is refused; the used set is per instance, kept until the token would expire); the `<Connect>` has no `action` attribute any more (the Redirect covers it). *Catalog tools:* `search_products` (at most three products, in stock, products only, positions the server maps to ids), `describe_product` (options with prices and stock, summary stripped of links and markup), run by the EXISTING public catalog handlers in process (`CatalogEndpoints.Listings` and `Listing`, made `internal`; no search or visibility logic is duplicated). *Cart tools:* `add_to_cart`, `view_cart`, `change_cart` use `CartService` unchanged (guest cart token held in the call state, or the account cart once verified in this call); the model passes positions and quantities only, never a price or an id (prices come from the catalog), the server refuses a bad quantity, an option not chosen, a sold-out option and anything over `Voice:MaxItems` (20) items; every cart change clears the quote and the confirmation (V-02). Money is said as speech (`six fifty`), tool answers carry `price_spoken` text. `set_guest_name` records a guest's name (2-60 characters, no digits or control characters). *Not in this slice:* address, delivery, quote (3), payment and order (4), status and cancellation (5), account setup and editing (6). | Brief V slice 2 and the security rules (codes by keypad only, caller ID is not authentication, the model proposes and the server decides). |
