| V-01 | 2026-11-17 | **Voice ordering: design note and slice 1 (skeleton).** *Where:* a module of the marketplace API (`Voice/`), not a sibling service: the API already has the catalog, cart, checkout, orders, notifier and staff area the tools must call, ASP.NET Core serves long-lived WebSockets natively, and a second deployable would duplicate auth, options and the outbox. It is OFF unless `Voice:Enabled` (start-up refuses a half-configured enabled module: `VoiceConfig.Problems`). *Flow:* SignalWire calls `POST /api/v1/voice/signalwire/inbound` (signed); we create a `voice_calls` row (idempotent on CallSid) and answer cXML: `<Say>` the AI disclosure (spoken by the platform, so it cannot depend on the model), then `<Connect action=after-stream><Stream url=wss://.../api/v1/voice/stream?token=...>`. The WebSocket endpoint checks a signed short-lived stream token (HMAC-SHA256, `{callId}.{expiry}.{mac}`, 120 s, key `Voice:StreamTokenKey`, its own key: start-up refuses it equal to `SignalWire:SigningKey`), then `RealtimeBridge` pipes G.711 mu-law 8 kHz audio both ways WITHOUT transcoding (`audio/pcmu` on the OpenAI side). When the stream ends SignalWire calls `after-stream` (signed): we answer `<Dial>` the support number if a transfer was requested and is possible, otherwise `<Hangup/>`. *Barge-in:* `input_audio_buffer.speech_started` while agent audio is playing sends `clear` to the phone and `conversation.item.truncate` (audio_end_ms from the bytes sent, 8 bytes per ms) upstream. *Latency:* speech_stopped to the first audio delta, one sample per turn, kept as sum and count per call. *Tools:* `IVoiceTool` (scoped, registered in DI), run by `VoiceToolRunner` on `response.done` (never while a response is active); an unknown name is `{"error":"unknown_tool"}`, bad JSON `bad_arguments`, an exception `tool_failed` (never its message). Slice 1 has `transfer_to_person` only; the transfer is offered only with `Voice:SupportNumber` set and inside `Voice:SupportHours` (fails closed on an unparsable value), otherwise the model is told it is unavailable. DTMF `0` on the stream is the same request. *Keypad:* every DTMF digit on the stream is dropped (never forwarded, never logged); later slices run codes and payment with `<Gather>`/`<Pay>` outside the OpenAI stream. *Model switch:* `Voice:ModelMode` full/mini/split; split buckets `SHA-256(callId) mod 100 < MiniPercent`, so a call keeps its model and the share is exact in the long run; model and tier are on the call row. *Cost:* the usage block of every `response.done` (`input_token_details`/`output_token_details`: audio and text tokens) is added to the row with atomic SQL increments; cost in whole micro-dollars (`tokens x Voice:PricePerMillionFull|Mini:AudioIn|AudioOut|TextIn|TextOut`, 0 until set); duration; latency; outcome (`in_progress` -> `transferred` if a transfer was made possible, else `abandoned`; `order_placed` arrives with slice 4). *Report:* `GET /api/v1/staff/voice/report?days=` with permission `voice.view` (finance, admin): per tier calls, cost per minute, average latency, order-completion, transfer and abandon percentages. *Limits:* `Voice:MaxCallSeconds` (900) ends a call; the other limits (`MaxOrderAmount`, `MaxItems`, `MaxOrdersPerDay`) are configuration now and enforced by the order tools in slices 3-4. *Logs:* call id, masked caller (`***4567`), tier, error CODES; never audio, digits, payloads, addresses, keys. *No transcripts, no audio, no recordings:* we never enable transcription events and store nothing of the conversation. *Retention:* call rows 400 days (`Voice:CallRetentionDays`, purged by the outbox worker's maintenance). *Model change:* table `voice_calls` (InitialCreate regenerated). **Verified on 2026-11-17 (docs read with curl; WebFetch cannot resolve the hosts):** (SignalWire, https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice/stream.md) a bidirectional stream is `<Connect><Stream url="wss://...">` (only `wss`); messages `connected`, `start` (`start.streamSid`, `start.mediaFormat`), `media` (`media.track` is `inbound`, `media.payload` base64), `dtmf` (`dtmf.digit`, with `streamSid`), `mark` (`mark.name`, echoed when played or cleared), `stop`; we send `media`, `mark`, `clear` with `streamSid`; default codec `PCMU@8000h` (mu-law, 8 kHz); `authBearerToken` makes SignalWire send `Authorization: Bearer` on the upgrade (we use it AND keep the token in the URL; the docs do not say whether a query string survives). **Not in the docs:** the Connect page (`.../voice/connect.md`) lists no `action` attribute, so the cXML also carries a `<Redirect>` to `after-stream` after the `<Connect>` (both reach the same idempotent endpoint); that SignalWire posts `CallSid` to a Redirect URL is the Twilio-compatible behaviour and is still unconfirmed on a real call. (Signature) the form-webhook scheme is `base64(HMAC-SHA1(signing key, URL + each parameter name and value sorted by name))`, read from the source of `@signalwire/compatibility-api` 3.2.1 (`lib/webhooks/webhooks.js`: it also tries the URL with and without the default port; ours signs exactly `Voice:PublicBaseUrl` + path, so configure it with no explicit port); JSON webhooks and the payment connector use the lowercase-hex form over URL + raw body (https://signalwire.com/docs/compatibility-api/cxml/reference/voice/pay.md, https://signalwire.com/docs/platform/webhooks.md): not used by this slice. (OpenAI, https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/realtime-conversations.md, voice-websockets.md, models/*.md, your-data.md) `wss://api.openai.com/v1/realtime?model=<id>` with `Authorization: Bearer`; `session.update` with `session.type = "realtime"`, `output_modalities: ["audio"]`, `audio.input.format` / `audio.output.format` `{type: "audio/pcmu"}` (also `audio/pcm` + `rate`), `audio.input.turn_detection` `{type: server_vad|semantic_vad, create_response, interrupt_response}`, `audio.output.voice`, `tools`; events `response.output_audio.delta` (`item_id`, `delta`), `response.done` with `response.output[]` items of type `function_call` (`name`, `call_id`, `arguments`) and `response.usage` (`input_token_details.{audio_tokens,text_tokens,cached_tokens}`, `output_token_details.{audio_tokens,text_tokens}`), `input_audio_buffer.speech_started|speech_stopped`, `conversation.item.create` with `function_call_output`, `conversation.item.truncate` (`item_id`, `content_index`, `audio_end_ms`; the server truncates by itself only on WebRTC/SIP, so on WebSocket we do it). Models on `v1/realtime`: **`gpt-realtime-2.1`** (full; audio $32 in / $64 out, text $4 in / $24 out per million tokens) and **`gpt-realtime-2.1-mini`** (audio $10 / $20, text $0.6 / $2.4); `gpt-realtime` and `gpt-realtime-mini` are marked **deprecated**, so the defaults are the 2.1 pair and the prices above are the defaults of `Voice:PricePerMillion*`. Voices: alloy, ash, ballad, coral, echo, sage, shimmer, verse, marin, cedar (OpenAI recommends marin or cedar; the docs do not state gender; default `cedar`, the owner picks by listening). Data: `/v1/realtime` is not used for training, abuse-monitoring logs keep content up to 30 days, application state is not stored, and it is Zero-Data-Retention eligible once approved for the organisation (request ZDR or Modified Abuse Monitoring from OpenAI: owner action). **Open for the owner:** OpenAI now also offers *GPT-Live* (`wss://api.openai.com/v1/live/sessions`, `session.start`, partner integrations incl. Twilio) as its recommended voice API; this build uses the Realtime API as the brief says (GA, still supported); moving later is a connector change, not a redesign. **Still to check on a real call (opt-in manual test):** SignalWire's query-string handling on the `wss` URL (the bearer header covers it), the Redirect after the stream, DTMF events while a bidirectional stream is active, and that `audio/pcmu` is accepted as an INPUT format (the docs show it for output). | Brief V (owner-approved 2026-11-17). Separate module: no second deployable. One key per purpose and a platform-spoken disclosure: security rules 3-5. |

| M6-10 | 2026-11-05 | **Azure issue #4: one unreadable portal listing no longer stops the catalog sync (model change: table `catalog_quarantine`, InitialCreate regenerated).** *Cause:* a listing announced by an event is fetched alone (`GetListingAsync`); when its document could not be mapped (null) the source threw `InvalidDataException`, the run's catch-all ended the WHOLE run, and because the back-off line was never reached the same rows stayed first in the queue on every run, so nothing behind them was ever applied (the feed page loop already skipped bad documents; the single fetch did not). *Fix:* `HttpCatalogSource.MapCore` says WHY a document cannot be read with a short code (`required_field`, `price`, `quote_price`, `seller`, `brand`, `category`), the feed page hands its unreadable documents to the sync (`ListingPage.Unreadable`) and a single fetch throws the typed `UnreadableListingException` (its message and the logs hold the id and the code, never the document). The sync SETS THEM ASIDE (`CatalogQuarantine`, one row per listing: reason, version, first and last seen, times seen): a listing we already hold is HIDDEN (status `Quarantined`, so `Visible` is false until a readable version arrives; the next applied snapshot restores it by itself), a pending fetch is backed off like any failing one (1, 2, 4 ... minutes, at most 6 h) and the run goes on, so the listings behind it are fetched. The row is deleted when the listing applies readable, is removed or is unlisted. `/health` shows `catalog.unreadable` (a gauge: the number of rows, refreshed after each run by the instance that holds the lease; alert when it rises). A portal OUTAGE (5xx, timeout, a non-JSON answer) is unchanged: it still fails the run and is retried. *A listing without a category* (the portal sends `category: null`): **hidden, not an "Uncategorized" bucket**: a catalog page, a facet and a breadcrumb all hang on the category, a synthetic bucket would show unfinished (here: demo) listings to buyers and need its own tree entry, and a hidden one costs nothing, comes back by itself when the portal sends a version with a category, and is visible on `/health` and in the table. *Quote-priced services:* the portal's `CatalogReader` sends `price: { amountMinor: null, currency }` and `service.pricingModel: "quote"` (docs/CATALOG.md does not describe the price of a quote service: to be added there). The read model keeps prices as numbers that drive sorting cursors, price filters, facets and every card, so a service WITHOUT a price needs a model decision (nullable price or a `priceMode`) and the web's display: that is a slice of its own. Until then such a service is set aside with its own reason `quote_price` (counted, hidden, restored when supported or priced), never mistaken for a broken document. | Azure issue #4; orchestrator task of 2026-11-05. |
| M6-11 | 2026-11-05 | **Proposal, queued as its own slice after c17-align: "M6 quote-priced listings".** Today a service the portal prices by quote (`service.pricingModel: "quote"`, `price.amountMinor: null`) is set aside under the reason `quote_price` (M6-10). To list it, the read model needs: (1) a price that can be absent (`priceFromCents`/`priceToCents` nullable, or a `priceMode: fixed|quote` with the numbers kept at 0) and the rules that follow: such a listing has no place in price sorts (after every priced listing in both directions, with a stable cursor key), is excluded from price filters and from the price facet's range, and never gets a cart line or a booking hold (the portal answers `422 quote_required` to a direct booking, the buyer asks for a quote through the existing quote-request flow, which needs only a visible service listing); (2) the API listing card and detail carry the mode so the web shows "Request a quote" instead of a price; (3) the web cards, the page and the structured data (no `Offer` price) follow; (4) the portal documents the shape in docs/CATALOG.md (the orchestrator asked them). | M6-03 | 2026-11-05 | **Review of the lists (H1, MED-1..3, L1-L3, L5).** (H1, high) The wishlist notification keys were `wishlist:{listing}:{version}:{kind}`, and the dedupe key is unique over ALL notifications: with two buyers on one listing the second insert failed the catalog save and the sync SKIPPED the snapshot, so the most-wished listings kept an old price or "sold out". Now the key holds the ACCOUNT (`wishlist:{account}:{listing}:{version}:{kind}`), and the catalog sync no longer writes notifications at all: it queues one `wishlist.watch` outbox message per applied visible listing version (`TryEnqueueAsync`, insert-unless-taken, so it cannot fail the save); `WishlistWatchHandler` does the work later in its own transactions, ONE BUYER AT A TIME, and a buyer whose write fails is logged (type only) and skipped with their baseline unchanged, never stopping the others or the catalog. Checkout reads prices from the same catalog row, so under H1 it too would have kept the old price for such a listing until the next version; it does not any more. (MED-1) The baseline is the lowest price over ALL variants (`WishlistWatchHandler.LowestPriceAsync`, also used when an item is added): a cheaper variant coming back in stock is no drop; a restock is reported as back-in-stock only when the listing was unavailable. (MED-2) At most one mail per kind per buyer and listing per 7 days (`LastPriceNotifiedAt`, `LastStockNotifiedAt` on `wishlist_items`; model change, InitialCreate regenerated); the baseline still moves each time, so a drop inside the cooldown is not announced later. (MED-3) Demo listings and listings that are not on the storefront now (`CatalogEndpoints.Visible`: seller inactive, unlisted) are skipped and leave the baseline alone. (L1) The shared view no longer carries `addedAt`. (L2) The limit checks are fail-closed races: after the insert the count is read and the row that went over is removed, so two parallel requests can both remove theirs and leave a little room, but the cap is never exceeded (tests: parallel adds to a 200-item list, 30 parallel lists). (L3) Only a unique violation answers `409 name_taken` / is read as "already added"; any other database error is a 500. (Follow-ups of the second review) The watch message is no longer inserted outside the snapshot's transaction: `WishlistWatchQueue` adds it to the SAME unit of work as the snapshot (page path and pending-fetch path alike), so it exists exactly when the snapshot is saved, a worker never reads it against old data, and a failed save leaves no used-up key (test: applied but not saved = no message; saved = one). The per-buyer isolation catches every failure except cancellation (type logged only). A price drop that the cooldown suppresses is NOT sent later (the baseline has moved on); `lastPriceDropNotifiedAt` and `lastBackInStockNotifiedAt` are in the data export. Tests: the notification parameters (`priceCents`, `previousPriceCents`), rise then a drop after the cooldown is announced, the 200-cap test needs at least one 204 and at least one 422. (Second review) The per-buyer isolation is narrowed: only a buyer's OWN failed write that is not a transient fault (`DbUpdateException` that `WishlistWatchHandler.IsTransient` rejects: timeouts, I/O and socket errors, provider errors marked transient, EF's "likely a transient failure" wrapper) is skipped. Anything else (a transient fault, the read of who wishes for the listing, a bug) is logged at Error with the exception type only and the message is RETRIED (`wishlist_watch_<type>`): safe, because the buyers finished so far have their baselines moved and the notification key holds account + listing + version, so a retry cannot tell anybody twice (tests inject a timeout into the notification insert and into the read, expect one retry with the message not delivered, then both buyers told exactly once after the backoff; a constraint failure still skips just that buyer). a decision is matched on the target WE reported (`ReviewReport.TargetId`: the review id, or the reply id), so it still matches after the reply is gone, a forged report id or target is skipped, and only the reported reply is taken down (a newer reply with another id stays); the review itself and its figures are untouched. **MED-1** every writer of a review reads it UNDER the review's row lock (`ReviewService.LockAsync`: a no-op UPDATE inside the transaction, then a fresh read): edits, moderation decisions, account erasure and photo uploads, so parallel edits (3 to 5 and 3 to 1) and an edit racing a hide leave every figure equal to what the published reviews add up to (the invariant is asserted by recomputing it). **MED-2** erasing an account no longer deletes the reports it made: they are anonymised (note dropped, account id replaced by a fresh id that belongs to nobody, a fixed empty id would collide on the one-report-per-buyer index) so staff decisions arriving later still apply; the export finds nothing by the old id. **MED-3** review photos: `Cache-Control: public, max-age=300, must-revalidate` plus an ETag (was a day): the visibility check runs before any 304, so a hidden review's photo is a 404 even for a cache that holds it; the web proxy route must pass the same header (web follow-up in M6 web). **L1** refund policy: a line refunded in full cannot be reviewed (422, and it is not offered), a partly refunded one can; a refund AFTER the review leaves the review as it is (it is the buyer's account of what they received) and editable; pinned by a test. **L2** review writes (create, edit, votes, reports, photos) are limited per signed-in account, `RateLimits:ReviewWritesPerMinute` (30), counted after the sign-in check. **L3** the question throttle counts and inserts under a lock on the buyer's account row. **L4** a review restored by staff is sent to the portal again (idempotent; a review hidden before its first send had never been sent), and a reply that arrives while the review is hidden is stored (not shown), because the ordering watermark has advanced and the portal will not resend it; a removed review keeps none. **L5** a helpful vote can be taken back from a hidden review (adding one still needs a published review). **L6** was M6-07. **L7** the photo status and edit window are re-read under the same lock (no deterministic test: the early check already refuses the plain cases; the race is the lock itself). Also: a sequence whose scope is not `review:{reviewId}` is ignored and logged as a warning. | Orchestrator review of 2026-11-05. |
| M6-08 | 2026-11-05 | **C17.1: the portal's `sequenceScope` and `sequence` on scoped events (accepted by the portal, being built by their worker P; field names to be checked against their example envelope).** Every scoped event (`so:…`, booking, `review:{reviewId}`) will carry two more top-level fields next to `id`, `type`, `createdAt`, `data`: `sequenceScope` (string) and `sequence` (integer that increases within the scope); unscoped events omit them. *Parsing:* `PortalEvent.TryParse` reads both for EVERY event type, only as a pair (a scope of 1..200 characters and a non-negative 64-bit integer); a missing, half-sent or malformed one means none (never a rejection of the event). *Ordering, review reply events (`review.reply_posted`, `review.reply_removed`):* when the event has a sequence and its scope is exactly `review:{reviewId}`, it is applied only if the sequence is GREATER than the last applied one in that review (`projection_states.LastSequence`, new nullable column, InitialCreate regenerated); an equal or lower one is skipped, whatever its time or event id (a dead event re-queued after its successors; the successors carry the later snapshot, and there is no read endpoint to reconcile with, so skipping is the reconcile). Still deduped on the signed `id` first. *Without a usable sequence* (legacy events, a malformed pair, another scope): ARRIVAL order, no longer createdAt + event id: the portal delivers one scope strictly in order, and ranking two edits of the same instant by a random id could lose the newer one. One guard stays: an event whose time is STRICTLY older than what was applied is still refused (a re-queued old event cannot overwrite a newer reply when it has no sequence to say so). Seller orders and bookings keep their createdAt + id ordering until a separate C17.1 slice adopts the same two store methods (`TryAdvanceBySequenceAsync`, `TryAdvanceByArrivalAsync`; `ProjectionContext.AdvanceBySequenceAsync`). | Brief M6; orchestrator message of 2026-11-05 (portal answer C17.1). |

Three differences found and fixed, each with a test: (1) **error bodies**: the portal's `422 invalid_review` sends `errors` as an object of field -> message, our `PortalError` expects the orders' array, so the whole error (its `code`) was lost and the dead letter read `portal_422_error`; the parser now keeps `code` and `message` when the rest does not fit, so it reads `portal_422_invalid_review` (this holds for every portal answer shaped like that). (2) **`predecessorDead` on `review.*` events** no longer logs the "seller order needs a person" alarm or marks the stored event: a reply event is a full snapshot (the text, or the removal), the later one says it all and there is no read endpoint to reconcile with, so nothing is left to do (booking events were already excluded; orders still reconcile). (3) **A reply report answered `404`** (the reply is unknown or already removed: the seller deleted it, or staff removed it, a moment ago) is Delivered, not dead-lettered: the thing reported is gone. Unchanged and now pinned by tests: a state `404` is Delivered (the portal never had the review, e.g. its creation was refused or a hidden review was never sent); `409 review_removed` and `422` are final; `429` waits 60 s (the shared 2000/min caller limit); `200 {duplicate:true}` and `{updated:true}` are success. The wire body has no `OrderKey` (it is the portal's outbox column), so reply events keep being ordered by the portal's `createdAt` plus the event id; two edits in the same millisecond could tie, which the portal's one-edit-at-a-time claim makes practically impossible. | Brief M6; portal C17 merge notice. |

Throttled BEFORE anything is stored: at most 3 questions per buyer per listing and 10 per buyer per day (`429 too_many_questions`; the portal's own limits are per (caller, listing) 60/min and 2000/min per caller, C6). The question is stored (`product_questions`, id `Q-<guid>` = our `questionId`) and its outbox message commits in the SAME save; the handler sends `POST /products/{id}/questions {questionId, text, buyerDisplayName}` (name = first name + last initial; the portal never publishes it): a duplicate delivery is the portal's `200 duplicate` (one question), 429 waits the minute it asks for, outages retry, and a `404` (listing no longer shown) or `422`/`409` ends the question as `rejected` (`listing_unavailable` / `invalid_question`) without retrying; a question deleted with the account before it was sent is never sent. The answer is NOT stored here: the seller's published answer comes back as part of the listing's Q&A in the catalog feed (already shown as the listing's FAQ). `GET /api/v1/account/questions` lists the buyer's own with their state. SET Count = Count + d`) in the same transaction as the review change (create, rating edit, hide, restore, erase), the row is created with insert-or-ignore first: parallel writers cannot lose an update (test: 6 parallel creates, 2 parallel edits, figures equal the rows). **Helpful votes:** one per buyer (composite key; insert-or-ignore decides whether the counter moves), never on one's own review, removable. **Reports:** `POST /reviews/{id}/report {reason, note?}` (and `/reply/report` for the seller's reply, `kind: review_reply`, target = reply id): one per buyer, sent through the outbox to the portal's `POST /content/reports` with an opaque reporter reference (hash of the account id) and OUR report id (`RV-...`). **Decisions** (`content.report_decided`, wired into M5's handler through `ReviewDecision`): the report id must be ours and the target that report's own review (or reply); the status follows all decided reports of the review (removed beats hidden beats published; a later `dismissed` of the same report restores a hidden review; removal is final and clears the text and reply); ordering by the event time per report. **Portal (C17, brief P-reviews-c17):** we send `POST /reviews` (idempotent; read at send time so a late message carries the latest text; limits 2000/60 checked before sending; nothing for demo rows) and `POST /reviews/{id}/state` (account deletion sends `removed`); the portal sends `review.reply_posted` / `review.reply_removed` (one reply per review, up to 2000 characters, kept by the portal's order, none on a review that is not shown). **Erasure:** reviews, the votes the account cast, the reports it made are deleted at once; figures and the helpful counts are taken back. Behind Azure the app always sees http, so telling an https request from an http one would need `X-Forwarded-Proto`, which this project deliberately does not trust (M8W-04); an HSTS header is meaningless on a server-to-server API. The control is Azure App Service "HTTPS Only" on the API app (plus restricting it to the web tier), listed in docs/API.md "Behind Azure"; if the owner wants an in-app guarantee, the sound way is a check of the proxy's own header with the proxy's address allow-listed, as its own decision with a test. | M7-01 | 2026-11-05 | **Staff are a separate account type, passkeys only.** Tables `staff_accounts`, `staff_passkeys`, `staff_sessions`, `staff_enrolments`, `staff_audit` (no buyer table is reused); the WebAuthn plumbing is the buyer's (Fido2NetLib, same relying party, user verification required) in `StaffPasskeyService`, with its own challenge purposes (`staff-enrol`, `staff-signin`, `staff-reauth`, `staff-addkey`) so a buyer's challenge can never be spent on a staff sign-in or the reverse. Session tokens are `stf_` + 256 random bits, stored as SHA-256 in `staff_sessions` and looked up ONLY there; the buyer filter looks only in `buyer_sessions`. A staff token is therefore `401` on `/api/v1/account/*` and a buyer token `401` on `/api/v1/staff/*` (tests both ways). A credential id already used by a buyer passkey is refused for staff. There is no password, no reset mail and no "remember me". Sessions: idle 30 min, absolute 8 h (`Staff:SessionIdleMinutes`, `SessionAbsoluteHours`); the account epoch ends every session at once (disable, role change, key revocation, sign-out-everywhere). | Brief M7: separate accounts, passkeys required, cookie never accepted as buyer. |

Sensitive actions (break-glass reveal, lock, refund retry, account management, adding or removing a passkey) additionally need a passkey check within `Staff:StepUpMinutes` (5): `403 reauthentication_required`, then `POST /staff/auth/reauthenticate`. | M7-03 | 2026-11-05 | **Audit.** Every staff action writes one `staff_audit` row in the SAME transaction as the action (`StaffAudit.Add` before the caller's `SaveChanges`): actor id and role, session id, dotted action, target type and id, reason, small structured detail, time. Reasons are 5-300 characters, required for everything that changes state or shows personal data, and REFUSED when they look like an email address or a run of 9+ digits (phone, card): staff write the ticket or order number. Rows hold ids only; no IP address is stored. Append only (no update or delete endpoint; the retention job is the only delete). Sign-ins, passkey checks and passkey changes are recorded (a failed anonymous sign-in is not: it has no actor, and the rate limit bounds it). Retention `Staff:AuditRetentionDays` (730, minimum 30). Admin reads it with `GET /api/v1/staff/audit` (keyset, filters on action, actor, target). | Brief M7 hard rule: who, what, why, when; PII never in the log. |

Registering the first passkey uses the enrolment up in the same transaction (a conditional UPDATE, so two parallel completions make one passkey); afterwards the token does nothing even if left in configuration (the start says so). Other members are created by an admin and get a one-time `ste_` link (shown once, hash stored); a lost device gets a new link, optionally revoking the old keys and sessions. CSRF: the API takes a bearer token, never a cookie; the web server holds the token in its own cookie and carries the CSRF check. | Brief M7: rate limited, optional IP allow-list, noindex. |
| M7-07 | 2026-11-05 | **Buyer lookup is masked and one at a time.** `POST /api/v1/staff/buyers/lookup` with `{ email | order | id, reason }` (M7-17 moved it from a GET query; exactly one key; no search by part of a name, so staff cannot browse buyers) and `POST /buyers/{id}/lookup { reason }` (an account id already known; also M7-17) return the account id, the email masked (`j***@e***.com`), the name as the sellers see it (`Jane B.`), the relay address (opaque), verified/locked/deleted/redacted flags, counts (passkeys, active sessions, addresses, orders) and `demo` (true when the account has demo data or a demo verification, M9). A lookup is an action: it needs a reason and is audited by the buyer's ID (or `buyer.lookup_miss` with no target), never by the text typed, so the log never gets an email. Support, Finance and Admin may look. | Brief M7: PII masked by default. |
| M7-08 | 2026-11-05 | **Break-glass reveal.** `POST /buyers/{id}/reveal {fields, reason}` shows the REAL value of only the fields asked for (`email`, `name`, `phone`, `addresses`), needs Support or Admin, a passkey check within 5 minutes and a reason, and is capped at `Staff:RevealsPerHour` (20) per staff member (counted from the audit log under the staff row's lock, so parallel calls count each other: `429 reveal_limit`). The audit row (which FIELDS, why, who, target id) is saved in the same transaction that reads the data, so a read that is not recorded cannot happen; it never holds the values. An erased account has nothing to show (`redacted: true`). | Brief M7: break-glass reveal audited. |
| M7-09 | 2026-11-05 | **Lock, unlock, sign out everywhere (buyers).** `BuyerAccount.StaffLockedAt` (**model change**, InitialCreate regenerated). Locking (Support, Admin; passkey check and reason) sets it, bumps the session epoch and revokes every session in one transaction: the buyer's tokens stop at once, password sign-in answers `403 account_locked` ONLY after the right password (a wrong one is the usual 401, so a stranger learns nothing), passkey sign-in answers the generic 401, and `AuthenticateAsync` refuses a locked account whatever token it holds. Unlocking clears it and never revives an old session. Lock and unlock are idempotent (a conditional UPDATE: parallel calls change it once and write one audit row). `POST /buyers/{id}/sign-out` ends every session without locking. No mail is sent to the buyer (the account may be in the hands of the person being locked out); the reason lives only in the audit log, not on the account. | Brief M7: lock/unlock, sign-out everywhere. |
| M7-10 | 2026-11-05 | **Orders and payments for staff, and refund retry through the existing handler.** `POST /api/v1/staff/orders/lookup { number, reason }` (`orders.view`; M7-17 moved it from a GET query) shows ONE order: number, status, money (`long` cents), the buyer by public name (`Jane B.`) or the guest email masked, ship COUNTRY only (no address, phone or gift note), seller orders, refunds with status and `retryable`, `demo`. The `payment` block (intent id, Stripe and portal refund ids, disputes with their last event and closed flag) is returned only to roles with `payments.view`; Support gets `null`. A lookup needs a reason and is audited by order id. `POST /buyers/{id}/orders { reason }` lists a buyer's last 50 orders (audited). **Retry** (`POST /orders/{id}/refunds/{refundId}/retry`, `refunds.retry`, passkey check, reason): the refund must be RESERVED (money counted, Stripe never confirmed) and its outbox message (`refund:<key>` or `refund:<key>:<attempt>`) DEAD; the message is put back in the queue and `StripeRefundHandler` runs it under the SAME Stripe idempotency key, so Stripe sees one refund however often it is clicked (test: four parallel clicks, one revive, one Stripe refund; a second round changes nothing). Staff code never calls Stripe. A refund Stripe REFUSED (rejected/failed) is final, its counters were given back: a new refund is a money decision made through the portal's cancel/refund, so it answers `409 not_retryable`. | Brief M7: Stripe status, failed refunds retry with reason through M3/M4's idempotent handlers. |
| M7-11 | 2026-11-05 | **Stuck ingests.** `GET /ingests` (`ingests.view`) lists orders still `paid` (not `placed`) more than `Staff:StuckIngestMinutes` (15) after payment, with the state of their `ingest:<number>` outbox message (status, attempts, error CODE, next attempt) and `retryable` (dead with its payload). `POST /ingests/{orderId}/retry` (`ingests.retry`: Support, Admin; passkey check, reason) revives the dead message once (a conditional UPDATE: parallel clicks revive it once); an order that is no longer `paid` is `409` (the handler would do nothing). | Brief M7: stuck ingests. |
| M7-12 | 2026-11-05 | **Outbox in the staff area.** `GET /outbox?status=&type=&limit=&before=` (`outbox.view`): counts and messages with type, status, attempts, error code, times and `retryable`; NEVER the payload (it can hold an address or a link). `POST /outbox/{id}/retry` (`outbox.retry`: Admin only; passkey check, reason; audited with the type). | M7-13 | 2026-11-05 | **Inbound dead letters.** The inbound store has no "failed" state (a handler error makes the webhook answer 5xx and the portal retries), so the dead letters are the PARKED events (their order, booking or quote is not known yet). `GET /dead-letters` (`deadletters.view`) shows the counts per status and the parked events oldest first (id, type, aggregate, age, note), never the body. `POST /dead-letters/retry {eventId, reason}` (`deadletters.retry`: Admin only; passkey check) runs the existing `ReplayParkedAsync` for that event's aggregate (each parked event is claimed by its row version, so a second click or a parallel sweep applies it once) and answers how many were applied. | Brief M7: dead letters view, retry. |
| M7-14 | 2026-11-05 | **Stripe webhook failures are a read-only view.** `GET /stripe/failures` (`stripe.view`: Finance, Admin) lists the Stripe events we stored whose outcome was `unmatched` (not an order of ours) or whose type says `failed`, newest first, with a keyset cursor. We keep only ids and types (no payload: it can hold card details), so there is nothing to replay: the fix is to resend the event from the Stripe dashboard (the idempotency table makes the resend safe). | M4-26 | 2026-11-05 | **Review of 2f0832d..30d450a.** MED-1: a released deferred refund that Stripe refuses because a NEW dispute opened (or while `DisputedCents > 0`) is flipped back to `Deferred` under the payment lock (its amount leaves the cap again) and the message is delivered, so the next release sends it; any other refusal is `Rejected`, given back and logged as an ALERT (`BookingRefundService.BounceAsync`). Every release sends under a fresh outbox dedupe key. MED-2: tests for the cap check of a release (lost dispute: the upkeep and a direct release pay nothing, `RefundedCents <= Captured - Disputed`; the cap-check mutant is killed). LOW-2: a PostgreSQL-only test (hook `BookingHooks.AfterPaymentRead`) shows a dispute update waits for the refund's payment lock (on SQLite it returns: every writer is serialised there). LOW-3: the mail no longer promises the rest ("returned only if the money is left with us"). LOW-4: every upkeep step runs on its own (an exception in one is logged by type and never skips the others). LOW-5: the booking checkout's insert is retried when a failed insert left no row (SQLite lock under load; found by reading, the 500 did not reproduce in 14 runs). New: when a quote's hold lapses (the `booking.hold_expired` event, a confirm refused with `hold_expired` (the payment is refunded in full: M4-15), or the upkeep reading an overdue hold as expired) the quote goes back to `Quoted` with no booking (`QuoteReopen`), as at the portal, and can be accepted again; if the quote's own validity (`expiresAt`) has ended it becomes `Expired` instead. Each accept after a finished booking is a new attempt with a new booking id (attempts count the quote's FINISHED bookings, so a parallel tap is still the same attempt and a lapsed hold is never answered with the dead booking). The 30-minute payment window for quote bookings is KEPT: quotes accepted before the portal change keep already-Confirmed bookings that still need paying (`PaymentDueAt` is set only when the accept answer is not a hold). Accept errors stay as before (`quote_expired` also marks the quote Expired, `quote_not_open`, `slot_not_offered`, `slot_unavailable` leaves the quote open). | Orchestrator note of 2026-11-05; SERVICES.md §5, C13. |

| M4-17 | 2026-11-04 | **Review of M4-15 (R1-R3, L1-L3).** (R1) A `charge.refunded` is only reconciled when none of our own refunds on that payment is reserved and unconfirmed (younger than an hour: the event is retried with a 5xx), and the cumulative amount is Stripe's fresh read, so one of our refunds is never recorded as a dashboard refund. (R2) A refund (cancel, no-show, decline, void) is reserved for what a chargeback leaves available and the rest is **deferred** (`booking_refunds.Status = deferred`), reserved again by `ReleaseDeferredAsync` when the dispute is won or closed; any amount that can be neither reserved nor deferred is an ALERT, so a refund the buyer is owed is never silently dropped. (R3) The pre-portal check of the confirm handler always queues the late cancel at the portal (a 404/409 there is success), so an earlier confirm that the portal applied and whose answer was lost cannot leave a live, unpaid booking. The sale is reported only while the payment is still Captured: a void that wins the race has refunded it, and the booking confirmed meanwhile is cancelled at the portal; a cancel racing a confirm that reports the sale in between retries as a reported sale (two tries). (L1) A refund failure is matched by Stripe's id or the `refund_key` in its metadata (it can arrive before our own commit: the event is retried), and a failed refund is **re-issued once** under a new attempt key (`:retry1`, never reported to the portal again); a second failure is an ALERT. (L2) A dispute that arrives before the confirm has reported the sale is queued **held**; the confirm releases it. (L3) The H1 race test asserts the portal's exact status. The `ReloadAsync` before applying the confirm answer is belt and braces: without it the stale row's version token makes the save fail and `ApplySummaryAsync` answers false, which takes the same path, so that mutant is equivalent. The compare-and-swap on `ExternalRefundedCents` is covered by a parallel-event test whose race only shows on PostgreSQL (CI). | Orchestrator review of a597e57 (R1-R3, L1-L3). |
| M4-16 | 2026-11-04 | **Follow-ups after the money review.** (1) **Notifications** go through M5's `Notifier.AddBooking` (in-app always kept, email per the buyer's preferences, unsubscribe links), called inside the event's own transaction with the portal event id as the once-only key, for what happened TO the buyer: confirmed, declined, time proposed, rescheduled by the provider, cancelled by the provider, completed, reminder (a live booking at the reminded time only), extra charge requested, no-show; the buyer's own cancel or reschedule is not news. The hand-built reminder mail is gone. (2) **L2 reconciliation** reads Stripe's cumulative `amount_refunded` fresh (`GetAmountRefundedAsync`, M3's R1/R2 approach) instead of trusting the event's number, and waits (a 5xx so Stripe retries) while one of our refunds is reserved and not yet confirmed (younger than an hour), so a refund of ours can never be recorded as an external one. (3) **Quote accept as a hold (portal N8, C13):** the answer is told apart by its status, no flag: a `Hold` follows the normal hold, pay, confirm flow with the hold's own expiry and no unpaid-booking timer; a booking that is already Confirmed or Requested (older portal) keeps the 30-minute payment window and the `payment_not_completed` cancel, which the portal still treats as a buyer cancellation (a policy fee can be recorded against someone who never paid) until N8 is live. | Orchestrator follow-ups; M5 notifier; portal N8. |
| M4-15 | 2026-11-04 | **Booking money review fixes** (independent review of a1c53d2..0c24759; every case was reproduced by a probe and now has a test that fails on 0c24759). (H1) The confirm handler never confirms a booking we already show as over, and re-reads the row after the portal answers: if it closed meanwhile (hold expired, buyer cancelled) the payment is **voided and refunded in full without reporting a sale**, and the booking the portal confirmed anyway is cancelled there (`portal.booking-cancel-late`, retried). `ApplySummaryAsync` now says whether it applied; a finished row is never reopened by a portal ANSWER either. (H2) An extra charge reaches Paid only through one atomic update (`ExtraChargeState == Consented AND AdditionalChargeCents == amount`); a payment for a superseded, declined or already-paid request fails it and is refunded in full. A changed request and a decline queue cancellation (M3's retried `stripe.cancel`) of the open intents, and a new request for an amount whose earlier intent was cancelled gets a numbered fresh payment. (M1) `BookingEventHandler` refuses any event that would move a TERMINAL row (set by the buyer's cancel, the upkeep or the confirm handler, which the projection store never sees) except a repeat of the same end (the cancellation that triggers the refund) and the extra-charge request after `Completed`; so a stale `booking.confirmed` can no longer re-show the meeting link. (M2) A cancel, no-show or decline refund for a payment the portal never recorded (`ReportedToPortal` false: the confirm is still retrying) voids and refunds in full and reports nothing; an unreservable refund on a void is logged as an ALERT. (L1) A losing buyer's retry with the same key answers the stored `409 slot_unavailable` (or the stored refusal), never `200 Failed`. (L2) A dashboard refund is recorded once with a compare-and-swap on `ExternalRefundedCents` against Stripe-CONFIRMED refunds of ours only (a merely reserved refund can no longer hide it) and counts against the cap. (L3) `charge.dispute.*` for a booking intent goes to the portal with `marketplaceBookingId`, ordered like the order path, and `DisputedCents` holds the amount back from refunds while the dispute is open or lost. (L4) `IPaymentProvider.GetIntentAsync`: a stored intent is read back instead of created again (Stripe forgets idempotency keys after 24 hours); a second intent created in a race is cancelled, never stored over the first (test seam `BookingHooks`). (L5) The intents behind a lapsed, declined or cancelled booking are cancelled. | Orchestrator review of 0c24759 (H1, H2, M1, M2, L1-L5). |
| M4-14 | 2026-11-04 | **Availability review fixes** (independent review of eb27be3). (H1) The portal's 600 calls per service per minute are shared by every shopper, and a new cache key per request (postal code, coordinates, window, minutes) bypassed the cache, so a few addresses could push a service into 503 for everyone: there is now a **per-service token bucket in front of the portal call**, default 300 per minute (`Services:AvailabilityPortalCallsPerMinute`, 1 to 600), refilled continuously, spent by cache MISSES only (a cache hit or a joined in-flight call is free, so normal shopping never meets it); when empty the answer is `503 availability_unavailable` (never cached). Coordinates for the availability query are rounded to 2 decimals (about 1.1 km: the postal code decides the service area, and the booking re-validates the exact address). (M2) `from`/`to` more than 400 days ahead are `422` before any date arithmetic (they overflowed `DateTimeOffset`). (M3) Every public service endpoint, and holds and quotes, use the catalog's own visibility rule including an ACTIVE SELLER (`ServiceListings.Visible`). (L4) Only a 200 is cacheable (`public, max-age=15` / `60`), everything else is `no-store`; the documented staleness is up to about 45 s. (L5) Start-up refuses `Services:MaxWindowDays` outside 1..62, `DefaultWindowDays` outside 1..MaxWindowDays, `AvailabilityCacheSeconds` above 30 and a bucket outside 1..600, in every environment. (L6) A start listed more than once keeps every provider (slots are one per start and end). (L7) Tests for every surviving mutant: each part of the question in the key, the window, the 62-day cap on both sides, the shared call surviving the first caller's disconnect, visibility, the per-address rate limit, Cache-Control; the earlier "product is a 404" tests named a listing that does not exist in the fixtures and are fixed (`challah-classic`). | Orchestrator review of eb27be3 (H1, M2, M3, L4-L7). |
| M4-13 | 2026-11-04 | **Quotes** (`/api/v1/quotes`, signed-in buyer). Request: our `QT-…` row is inserted before the portal is called, `Idempotency-Key` unique per buyer and bound to a hash of the request (same key + other request = `409 idempotency_conflict`), at most 10 open requests per buyer, an at-customer request needs an address, preferred times must be future (max 5), portal answers are idempotent on our quote id; a lost answer is resolved by the upkeep sending the same request again (the portal answers `created:false`) and failed after a day. The provider's answer (`quote.sent`: price, duration, up to 10 offered times, expiry, note) is projected by `QuoteEventHandler` (matched on our id AND the portal quote id, parked until the portal id is stored, ordered by the portal's time so a newer quote replaces an older one in any arrival order, incomplete quotes ignored, the buyer is mailed once per event id); `quote.declined`/`quote.expired` and the buyer's own accept/withdraw are absorbing, and a late provider event never reopens a finished quote. **Accept** (`POST /quotes/{id}/accept { start, addOnIds?, intake?, phone? }`, no price field): the start must be one of the offered times, the quote not expired; our booking row (`HoldPending`, key `quote-accept:{quote}:{attempt}` so parallel taps are ONE booking and a retry after a refusal is a new attempt) is inserted before the portal creates the booking under its slot lock (`409 slot_unavailable` keeps the quote open so another time can be chosen; `quote_expired` closes it). **The portal confirms an accepted quote immediately (no hold), so the booking is Confirmed but unpaid:** it must be paid within `Bookings:QuotePaymentMinutes` (30, `PaymentDueAt`); checkout accepts such a booking until then, a payment skips the portal confirm (already confirmed) and only reports `payment.captured` (kind `booking`), and the upkeep cancels an unpaid one at the portal (`cancel-unpaid-{BK}`, reason `payment_not_completed`); a payment that lands afterwards is refunded (booking closed). Open point for the portal (asked via the orchestrator): accepting with a hold, or a free cancel for `payment_not_completed`, would avoid a policy fee being recorded against a buyer who never paid. | Brief M4 (quotes); SERVICES.md §5; M4-08. |

The catalog feed has no add-ons, intake form, provider names or policy (CATALOG.md), so the marketplace reads them from the portal's `GET /services/{id}/options` (contract request M4-C1, not shipped yet): cached 5 minutes and fetched once for a burst, in-flight call on its own cancellation scope; a portal that answers 404/405/501 is cached for a minute as `{available:false}` with empty lists (the widget then works without add-ons and intake questions, nothing is guessed), a failure is `503 options_unavailable` and never cached. The portal's document is cut down: lists bounded (50 add-ons, 30 questions, 100 providers), strings clipped, question kinds whitelisted (`text|longText|choice|yesNo|number`, else `text`), ids must be `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]`, percentages 0-100, unknown fields dropped, so nothing the portal sends reaches the buyer unchecked. | Brief M4 (add-ons, provider choice, intake). |

RFC 5545 with CRLF, escaped text and lines folded at 75 octets on character boundaries. It carries the title, booking number, UTC times, the kind of place and a link to the booking page; **no address, no meeting link, no personal data** (calendar files are forwarded and synced), `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`. | Brief M4 (.ics); portal ICS rule (SERVICES.md §10). |
| M4-09 | 2026-11-04 | **Money after cancellation, no-show, decline.** The buyer prepays the whole total, so a policy fee is NOT charged again: it simply stays inside the payment (the portal's `refundDueCents` is `total - fee`, SERVICES.md `BookingService`, and its ledger posts the sale of the total and refunds against it; a second `booking_cancellation_fee`/`booking_no_show_fee` capture would pay the seller the fee twice, so those kinds are not used in this flow). The refund is reserved by the `booking.cancelled` / `booking.no_show` / `booking.declined` projection in the SAME transaction as the event: buyer cancel = the event's `refundDueCents` (total minus fee when absent), seller cancel = everything (never a fee, whatever the event claims), no-show = `refundDueCents`, decline = everything left; always capped at what is left of the capture, keyed `{reason}:{BK}` so a repeated or second event cannot refund twice, and reported to the portal as `refund.processed` with `marketplaceBookingId`. The buyer's cancel request itself moves no money. **Fee preview** (`GET /bookings/{id}/cancel-preview`): a hold or a pending request is free; for a confirmed booking the fee is shown only when the portal has sent its policy snapshot (`policy` on the booking summary, a contract request): `round-half-up(total x cancelFeePercent / 100)` once less than `cancelFreeHours` remain, 0 after a seller reschedule; otherwise `feeKnown:false`, never a guess. The authoritative fee is always the portal's `cancellationFeeCents`. | Brief M4 (fees); handoff C9 lesson; portal `BookingService.Cancel`. |
| M4-10 | 2026-11-04 | **Buyer actions** (`BookingActions`): cancel, reschedule request, answer a proposed time, acknowledge a seller reschedule, additional-charge accept/decline. Each portal call is made while the buyer waits (30 s, O-08), idempotent on OUR booking id plus a request id we derive (`cancel-{BK}`; `resched-{BK}-{Idempotency-Key}`), so a retry or a double tap is the same request; the portal's answer (the summary) is stored, never the client's values (a reschedule names only the new start, which the portal validates). Refusals map to `409` (`invalid_state`, `slot_unavailable`, `reschedule_limit`, `too_late`, `request_expired`, `version_conflict`) or `422`, outages to `503 booking_unavailable`; portal text is never forwarded. A repeated cancel of a cancelled booking is `200`. `booking.reminder` mails the buyer once per event id, only for a live booking whose start still equals the reminded time. **Additional charge** (M4-06): `booking.additional_charge_requested` follows `booking.completed`, so it is applied as a completion fact (same terminal state, refused after any other terminal state); consent creates ONE intent per request amount (`pay:{BK}:extra:{amount}`), the amount is the stored portal amount and the body has none; a changed request needs a new consent; a payment above the portal's current cap, or for a cancelled booking, is refunded and never reported (the portal would refuse it); a paid amount is never replaced. | Brief M4; SERVICES.md §4, §6. |
| M4-02 | 2026-11-04 | **Holds** (`POST /api/v1/bookings/holds`, signed-in buyer, `Idempotency-Key` header required). The request has **no price, total or end time**; the only time the client names is the slot's `start`, which the portal re-validates (grid, free, notice) together with duration, add-ons and price. Order of work: validate, find the buyer's row for this key, cap open holds (3 per buyer, `Bookings:MaxActiveHolds`), **insert our row (`HoldPending`, `BK-…` id) before calling the portal**, then call `POST /bookings/holds` with that id and store the portal's answer (amounts, expiry, portal id, number). The key is unique per buyer and bound to a hash of the request: the same key and request is the same booking (200, `created:false`, no second portal call, parallel sends included); the same key with a different request is `409 idempotency_conflict`. **Exactly one buyer wins a slot: the portal decides** (its slot lock); the loser gets `409 slot_unavailable`, her row becomes `Failed` and is never shown (not in lists, 404 on fetch). An outage or timeout is ambiguous, so the row stays `HoldPending` and the buyer's retry (same key) resumes with the same `BK-…` id; the sweeper (M4-05) resolves it otherwise. A portal answer that names another booking or service is refused. The hold's amounts are the portal's `long` cents. | Brief M4; SERVICES.md §3, §6; handoff risks (parallel holds, idempotency, client-supplied values). |
| M4-03 | 2026-11-04 | **`booking.*` receiver handler** (`BookingEventHandler`, one handler for the 14 types; `message.sent` is M5's, `booking.reminder` and `quote.*` come in later M4 slices). Matched on OUR marketplace booking id AND the portal booking id (`EventRefs.Matches`); an event for a row whose portal id is not stored yet is **parked** and replayed when the hold answer is stored (tested with a lost answer). Status changes use the `status` field group; a proposed time, a reschedule request, an extra-charge request and a provider change have their own groups, so a late one is not lost behind a newer status event; all groups obey the terminal rule: Declined, Expired, CancelledBy*, Completed and NoShow are **absorbing** (O-10, no explicit exception), so no later event, however new, reopens a finished booking. Money in events is read as whole cents only (fractions, strings and values above 100 million dollars are ignored); a seller cancellation never carries a fee; `refundDueCents` is clamped to the booking total. A seller reschedule sets `RequiresBuyerConsent` and does not count against the buyer's reschedule limit; a buyer-initiated one counts. | Brief M4; CONTRACT §4; O-10; review hotspot (matching, ordering). |
| M4-04 | 2026-11-04 | **The meeting link** (`onlineInstructions`) is stored only while the booking is Confirmed, Rescheduled or InProgress and is cleared the moment it leaves them (cancel, decline, completion, expiry) and by retention. It is returned **only on the booking detail view of its own buyer**, never in a list, never for a hold or a request (the portal does not send it then either; a hold response that carries one is ignored), with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. It is never logged (a test greps the captured logs). | Handoff risk: "the meeting link never leaked to unconfirmed buyers or logs". |
| M4-05 | 2026-11-04 | **Booking upkeep** (`BookingWorker`, every minute, `BookingMaintenance`). A `HoldPending` row older than 60 s (`Bookings:PendingResolveAfterSeconds`) is looked up with `GET /bookings/{mid}`: found, the answer was lost, so it is applied and the parked events are replayed; 404, the portal never saw it, so it is `Failed (not_found)`; an outage ends the run and changes nothing; after a day it is `Failed (unresolved)`. A `Hold` more than 2 minutes past its expiry is re-read the same way (a lost `hold_expired` event cannot keep it held). **Retention (see the PII table):** 30 days (`Bookings:RedactAfterDays`, as the portal) after a booking ended, and 1 day after a failed hold attempt, the address, coordinates, phone, intake answers and meeting link are wiped; money, ids and statuses stay. | Brief M4; SERVICES.md §9. |
| M4-06 | 2026-11-04 | **Extra charges are requests.** `booking.additional_charge_requested` only records the amount and sets `ExtraChargeState = requested`; the booked total never changes and nothing is charged. A later (older) request cannot change an amount, and a paid state is never replaced. Charging needs the buyer's explicit consent on the booking page (payments slice) and never exceeds the stored `AdditionalChargeCents`. | Brief M4: "buyer consent required before charging". |
| M4-07 | 2026-11-04 | **Identity and ids.** The portal sees the buyer as `Jane B.` and the account's relay address only (`RelayIdentity`), never the real email or full name; tests assert the real address never appears in a body sent to the portal. Our booking id is `BK-` plus 16 characters of a 32-letter alphabet from a CSPRNG (19 characters, inside the portal's `[A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{1,64}` rule), unique per row; the buyer-facing id is our own GUID, so the portal's number and our `BK-` id are never needed in a URL. | Contract §8; SERVICES.md §6. |
| M4-08 | 2026-11-04 | **Paying for a booking** (own tables `booking_payments`, `booking_refunds`; M3's `IPaymentProvider`, outbox and portal payment events are reused, order tables and code untouched except a three-line dispatch in `StripeEventProcessor` for intents whose metadata has `marketplace_booking_id`). Flow: hold, `POST /bookings/{id}/checkout` (intake answers and phone stored, **one payment per booking** keyed `pay:{BK}:booking` which is also Stripe's idempotency key, amount = the portal's `totalCents`, the body has no amount), Stripe `payment_intent.succeeded`, then the hold is confirmed at the portal through the outbox (`portal.booking-confirm`, retried on outage) and only then is `payment.captured` (kind `booking`, `marketplaceBookingId`, `paymentRef` = intent id) queued once. **Never captured silently:** a payment for a booking that is already over (hold expired, declined, cancelled), with an amount or currency other than the one priced, or that the portal refuses to confirm (409 hold lapsed and slot taken, 422, 404) is voided, **refunded in full** through the reserve-before-call refund service (`RefundedCents + amount <= CapturedCents`, unique key, so no parallel or repeated path can refund twice or above the capture), never reported to the portal as a sale or a refund (it never recorded the sale), and the buyer is mailed. A refund that reverses a reported sale is reported as `refund.processed` with `marketplaceBookingId`. A refund made in the Stripe dashboard is recorded against the cap and logged for a person. Fees, no-show fees and extra charges reuse these tables with their own kinds and keys in the next slice. | Brief M4 payments; handoff (hold expiry, never charge twice, M3 lessons O-18). |
| M4-01 | 2026-11-04 | **Availability proxy** (`GET /api/v1/services/{listingId}/availability`, public, visible `service` listings only; the listing id is the portal service id). The window is snapped to whole UTC days (`from` floored, `to` rounded up, `from` never before today) and is at most 62 days, so shoppers asking the same thing share one cache key. Key = service, window, location, postal code (upper-case), provider, lat/lng (4 decimals), minutes, add-ons (sorted, distinct). Answers are cached at most 30 s (`Services:AvailabilityCacheSeconds`, clamped to 30) and **single-flighted**: concurrent identical requests share one portal call, which runs on its own cancellation scope (one shopper leaving never cancels the others' answer) and is bounded by the client's 30 s total deadline (O-08). Failures and portal 4xx answers are shared with the callers already waiting but never cached. Portal errors are translated (`404 not_found`, `422 outside_service_area|location_not_offered|duration|add_on|provider_required`, everything else `503 availability_unavailable`); portal messages are never forwarded. Slots are the portal's UTC instants, sorted and de-duplicated; the provider's IANA zone is passed through for display and nothing is re-zoned here (DST is the portal's slot engine's job; tested across the 2027 spring-forward). The proxy is advisory: a booking re-validates start, duration and price at the portal and the client's values never decide them. | Brief M4; CONTRACT §4; handoff (rate limit per service). |

| M1-13 | 2026-11-04 | **Logging (orchestrator review 1.1, extended).** `appsettings.json` sets `Microsoft.AspNetCore` and `System.Net.Http.HttpClient` and `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore` to Warning and `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Hosting.Diagnostics` to **None**. Warning alone is not enough: the hosting diagnostics logger also attaches a `RequestPath:` logging **scope** (full path, so `/verify/<email>/<token>`) to every log line written during a request, including our own, and scope-aware providers (JSON, OTel, App Insights) would record it; the scope is only created while that category is enabled. The web server forwards the end-user IP in `X-Solvelt-Client-Ip`, `X-Solvelt-Client-Timestamp` (unix seconds, +-60 s since M1-28) and `X-Solvelt-Client-Signature: v1=hex HMAC-SHA256(Web:ForwardingKey, "<timestamp>\n<ip>")`. The timestamp window is 60 s (M1-28). `Web:ForwardingKey` is its own key (env `Web__ForwardingKey`, >= 32 chars, required in Production, must differ from both portal secrets); a dedicated signed header was chosen over `ForwardedHeaders`/`KnownProxies` because the web tier's addresses are not fixed. A missing, malformed, stale or wrongly signed set is ignored and the request is keyed by the socket address (`peer:`), so a client cannot pick or rotate its bucket; a null remote address maps to `peer:unknown` instead of throwing. IPv6 collapses to its /64 and IPv4-mapped IPv6 to IPv4. Buckets are per (policy, end user), so `auth` and `public` never share. Documented in `docs/API.md` for W1. | Review hotspot: one abuser must not lock everyone out. |

An attempt is bounded by `min(Portal:AttemptTimeoutSeconds (clamped 1..30), time left)`; the next attempt starts only if its backoff still fits (`elapsed + backoff < total`), otherwise the last answer is returned (`Retryable`) or `PortalUnavailableException` is thrown. Longer persistence is the outbox's job (ingest and payment events go through our outbox with its own backoff).
