| M5-22 | 2026-11-04 | **Seller images are re-encoded; no virus scanner yet (independent review G2, G6; replaces the container-stripping parsers of M5-13 for images).** A JPEG, PNG or WebP is decoded and a fresh image of the same format is encoded with SkiaSharp (MIT; ImageSharp is not licensed for commercial use), so nothing hidden in a container survives: EXIF, XMP, MPF and secondary images, GPS, comments, data after the end of the image. The EXIF orientation is applied first (all eight), the longer side is capped at 4096 px, and a picture of more than 40 million pixels is refused from its header, before any pixel is decoded; the result is capped at 5 MB; a file that does not decode is `malformed_<type>`. Animated images keep their first frame. PDFs keep the active-content refusal. There is NO virus scanner for now: every sanitized file is offered to `IAttachmentScanner` before it is stored (the default allows; a refusal is `rejected`), and files are still served only as downloads with `nosniff` and a sandboxing CSP. | Review G2, G6. |
| M5-23 | 2026-11-04 | **Message matching and limits (review G1, G3, G4, info).** A seller reply on a product thread needs our conversation id, a valid `productId` equal to the thread's listing AND a `sellerId` that owns that listing; an order reply needs `sellerOrderNumber` (our order number alone never matches, a single-seller order included). Sending and reporting messages is limited per SIGNED-IN ACCOUNT (`RateLimits:MessagesPerMinute`, 20), counted by an endpoint filter after the sign-in check, so requests before sign-in count for nothing and two buyers behind one address do not share a bucket (the per-address `Public` limit still applies in front). A thread is paged by `(SentAt, Id)` (`before` + `beforeId`, the response has `older` and `olderId`). A message hidden or removed by staff loses its text (not only its display). `Notifications:PublicApiBaseUrl` is required in production and must be an https URL. | Review G1, G3, G4, info. |
| M5-24 | 2026-11-04 | **SkiaSharp in production; licences (orchestrator, review G2).** The app references `SkiaSharp` 3.116.1 and `SkiaSharp.NativeAssets.Linux.NoDependencies` 3.116.1 (no system packages needed: slim and Alpine-like images work). At start the app decodes and re-encodes an embedded 1x1 PNG (`AttachmentSanitizer.SelfTest`); if that fails it does not start in Production (a warning in Development/Testing). Licence check: SkiaSharp is MIT; Google's Skia and its bundled natives (libpng, libjpeg-turbo, libwebp, zlib, FreeType, HarfBuzz) are BSD-3-Clause or similarly permissive (zlib, libpng licence); none is copyleft, all allow commercial use. ImageSharp is not used (split commercial licence). Keep the attribution notices in the product's third-party licence file when one exists. | Orchestrator note on G2. |
| M5-25 | 2026-11-04 | **Replay safety and transactional mail footers (review X1, X2, X3, X7).** The outbox dispatcher clears the change tracker before every message and after a failed one (one scope serves a whole drain, so a failed handler's half-applied entities can never be saved by the next); `ReprojectHandler` also clears it in its own catch. A replay writes a marker row `ProjectionStates("reproject", <event id>, "done")` in the SAME transaction as the projection and does nothing when it exists (the outbox is at-least-once; additive counts such as a partial cancel have no ordering guard of their own). Transactional mail (O-21) carries NO unsubscribe link and NO `List-Unsubscribe` header (there is nothing to unsubscribe from; the old link used a token the API rejects): its footer says the mail is required for orders and the account and points to the email settings page. The subject cut at 200 characters never splits a surrogate pair. | Review X1-X3, X7. |
| M5-26 | 2026-11-04 | **Portal N3: ids per seller, reports, retention.** Message ids are unique per seller only, so our own outbound message ids are `MG-` + 24 hex characters of a fresh GUID, unique across sellers, so an ambiguous answer cannot happen for them (the portal confirmed it reads neither `sellerId` nor `sellerOrderNumber` on receipts and reports, so we do not send them; only `POST /messages` takes `sellerOrderNumber`). `422 ambiguous_message` / `ambiguous_target` (and any other 4xx) are permanent: the message is dead-lettered after ONE attempt, which logs an error (the alert). `409 already_resolved` on a report is done. Inbound events are deduplicated on the signed body id (the header must equal it, else 400). Retention of report free text: the buyer's reason and note live only in the outbox payload, purged 1 day after delivery and 30 days when dead (stricter than the portal's 90, ours kept); the staff's `reasonShownToUser` (`ModerationNote`) is cleared 90 days after the decision (`FulfilmentMaintenance`), the message stays hidden. | Portal N3. |
| M5-27 | 2026-11-04 | **Deleting an account (review G5; M1's `IAccountEraser`).** `MessagingAccountEraser` runs first in the redaction job: it deletes the account's notifications and email preferences, and redacts ALL its message threads at once, whatever state the order is in (an order thread of a seller order that never ends would otherwise keep its texts for ever): texts emptied, attachment rows and bytes deleted, the thread closed. Idempotent, so a retried job is safe. Orders, lines and money stay (accounting; M3 owns them). The PII table's message rows now say "with the account" too. | Review G5. |
| M5-28 | 2026-11-05 | **Review of the account/eraser work (Q1, P1, P2) and portal ordering (C14/N12).** Q1: `AfterSalesAccountEraser` (module "order-cancellations-and-returns", now out of the PII registry's pending list) clears, inside M1's erasure transaction, the free text of cancel requests and returns (note, comment, seller decision notes), the return's client idempotency key and its label links; status, reason code, quantities, ids and dates stay. P1: a seller message for an order of an ERASED account is skipped (no thread), and the projection creates no notification (so no mail) for an erased account. P2: images are decoded within a 16-million-pixel budget (about 64 MB): a JPEG or WebP over it is decoded at 1/2, 1/4 or 1/8 size, a PNG (which cannot be reduced while decoding) is refused; the header limit is 64 MP; one attachment is processed at a time (a process-wide gate). Portal C14/N12: events of one seller order arrive in order; a top-level `predecessorDead: true` means our view of that order has a gap. The contract has no `GET` for a seller order, so M5 cannot reconcile by reading it today: our projections are order-independent (ordering guards, terminal rules, the replay) and the event is applied as usual; a portal read endpoint for seller orders would let us add `portal.order-reconcile` like M4's `portal.booking-reconcile`. We keep deduplicating on the signed body id (a re-queued dead event arrives late). | Review Q1, P1, P2; portal C14/N12. |
| M5-29 | 2026-11-05 | **`portal.order-reconcile` (portal C16 / N13; refines M5-28).** An order event flagged `predecessorDead: true` queues ONE `portal.order-reconcile {marketplaceOrderId}` (deduped on the signed event id) from the projection. The handler reads the portal's view (`GET /api/internal/v1/orders/{mid}`, typed client `ISignedPortalClient.GetOrderStateAsync`, models `PortalOrderState` / `PortalSellerOrderState`, no personal data) and brings the FULFILMENT state up to it in one transaction: line counters (shipped, cancelled, refunded, returned) only ever go UP and are clamped to the ordered quantity; a seller order's status only moves FORWARD (new, accepted, on hold, partially shipped, shipped, delivered) or into cancelled/refunded, and never leaves a final state; missing shipments are added (carrier, tracking number, shipped/delivered times, never in the future). Money (refunds, `refundedCents`) is never touched: M3 owns the comparison and the alert. Parked events of the order are replayed afterwards. 404, 405 and 501 (the portal cannot answer yet) are logged as an error (the alert; no ids) and the message is done, not retried; a timeout, 5xx or unreachable portal is retried with backoff; other 4xx are dead-lettered. The event that carried the flag is still applied as usual. | Portal C14/N12, C16/N13. |
| M5-30 | 2026-11-05 | **Guest-link order view (shapes agreed with M3, M3-24/M3-32).** `GET /api/v1/guest-orders/{number}` with the order-access link in `X-Guest-Order` (M1's `GuestLinks`; no sign-in). BEFORE a claim the guest sees the progress of the order per seller order: status and its dates, lines with quantity and shipped quantity, shipments (carrier, tracking number and link, shipped/delivered times, tracking status, ETA), amounts and `refundedCents`; NEVER the delivery address, phone, gift note, buyer name, guest email, driver position, proof of delivery, returns/refund rows or any action (no cancel, return or confirm). AFTER a claim (`ClaimedAt` set or an account on the order) only `{ number, status, createdAt, claimed: true }`. No link or a forged/expired one is a 401; a genuine link for another order, or an unknown or unpaid number, is a 404. `private, no-store`, per-address `Public` rate limit. | Brief: guest orders; M3-24, M3-32. |
| M5-31 | 2026-11-05 | **Booking messages.** A third thread kind, `booking`: one thread per buyer and booking (`message_threads.BookingId`, unique with the account). The buyer sends with `POST /api/v1/messages { marketplaceBookingId: "BK-…", text }` (same endpoint, contact-details warning and per-account limit as the other kinds); only for the buyer's OWN booking that the portal already knows (a hold the portal has not answered has no portal id: 404). It goes out through the outbox as `POST /bookings/{mid}/messages { messageId (MG-…), conversationId, buyerName, text, sentAt }` (SERVICES.md §6). A seller's `message.sent` with `kind: "booking"` is matched on OUR marketplace booking id AND the portal's booking id (`EventRefs.Booking`/`Matches`, the `booking` object of the event) and on the booking's own account; the conversation id, when sent, must equal the thread's; a booking whose portal id is not stored yet PARKS the message (replayed with the booking's events); an erased account's booking drops it; a seller may start the thread. Delivery and read receipts are NOT sent for booking messages and a booking message cannot be reported through `/content/reports` (the portal's own endpoint for booking-message reports, `POST /booking-messages/{id}/reports`, is not wired yet: 422 `not_supported`). Retention: like a product question, 90 days after the last message (texts emptied, files deleted, thread closed), and at once when the account is deleted (M5-27). The inbox and thread views carry `marketplaceBookingId` so the website can link to the booking. | Brief: booking messages; CONTRACT §4. |
| M5-32 | 2026-11-05 | **"Download my data".** `POST /api/v1/account/data-export` (signed in; needs a sign-in no older than 10 minutes, the rule of deleting the account: else 403 `reauthentication_required`; `Auth` rate limit; `private, no-store`; `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename=solvelt-my-data.json`) returns `{ generatedAt, sections: { <module>: … } }`. Each module contributes a section through `IAccountDataExporter` (scoped service, called with the buyer's own id; no secrets, nothing about other people). M5 provides `account` (profile and address book; no password hash, tokens or sessions), `messages` (threads, texts, file names; hidden or redacted texts are empty), `notifications` (entries and the email settings) and `cancellations-and-returns`. **M3 (orders, refunds) and M4 (bookings, quotes) add their own sections by registering an exporter**; until they do, those sections are absent from the file. The file is generated on request, never stored. | Brief: privacy pages; GDPR/CCPA access. |
| M5-33 | 2026-11-05 | **The data export is audited, limited and complete (review of 4e1c7d2, MED-2/MED-3, L7, L8).** *Audit*: every export logs ONE info line (`Account data export: account <id>, sections orders=2, messages=5, …`: counts only, never content) and creates a `data_exported` notification (transactional, so the mail cannot be switched off: "Your data was downloaded from Solvelt", pointing at the privacy page, no data in it). *Limit*: one export per account per 10 minutes (`export_rate_limited`, 429 with `Retry-After`). The earlier export is read from its own notification, so there is no new table; the notification's unique key contains the 10-minute window, so two requests that both pass the check cannot both be answered (the second insert fails and is answered 429). The 10-minute limit equals the recent-sign-in window, so in practice a second export always needs a new sign-in as well. *Complete*: new sections `carts` (lines with item titles and quantities), `passkeys` (name, created, last used; NOT credential id, public key or counter) and `tracking` (per seller order: fulfilment state and the parcels with carrier, tracking number and carrier timeline; NOT the driver's name or position, proof links or portal ids); orders came from M3 (M3-44) and bookings from M4. Children are grouped with `ToLookup` in every exporter (no `Where` per parent). *Demo rows* (L7) are exported and marked `demo: true` (addresses, carts, orders, bookings, message threads, notifications), not hidden: the file says what we hold. *Notification parameters* (L8) are exported only for the keys `sellerName`, `subject`, `carrier`, `amountCents`, `bookingNumber`; our own ids are not in the file. *Coverage test*: every module of `PiiRegistry` is either mapped to sections in the file or in an explicit allow-list with a reason (demo marker rows; raw portal event bodies), and every registered exporter belongs to a module, so a new module or exporter cannot be added without deciding where it goes. *Large accounts*: the file is built in memory in one request. That is fine for the size of a buyer's data today (hundreds of rows). When an account can hold tens of thousands (many orders and long message threads), the plan is: stream the JSON section by section with `Utf8JsonWriter` straight to the response (each exporter returns `IAsyncEnumerable` instead of an object), and if that is still too slow, build the file in a background job, store it (encrypted, 7 days) and mail an expiring signed link; the endpoint, the limit and the audit stay as they are. | Orchestrator review MED-2, MED-3, L7, L8. |
| M5-34 | 2026-11-05 | **A redacted booking closes its message thread (review L4/L5/L6).** When a booking is redacted (its personal data erased) the maintenance job closes its message thread the same way it closes a thread after 90 days (texts emptied, files deleted, thread closed), not after 90 days of silence; before the job runs the thread already refuses buyer messages (409 `thread_closed`, by thread id; a booking id of a redacted booking is a 404), and a late seller reply is skipped (`booking redacted`, no thread, no notification). A seller reply for a deleted account is skipped (test added; the check had no test). The demo guard (`DemoMessageGuard`) refuses a message about a demo booking by booking id and by thread id (409 `demo_data`). The guest order view treats an order that has an account as claimed even without a claim time (test added). | Orchestrator review L4–L6, L9. |
| M2-17 | 2026-11-04 | **The portal feed is the default catalog source** (`HttpCatalogSource`, one file: the only place that knows the portal's document shape; `Catalog:Source=Http`, the default outside Development/Testing; `None` stays available; the validator now accepts `Http`). The client gains four signed, read-only GETs (listings page, one listing, categories, brands) with a 16 MB response cap for them (the default 2 MB stays for everything else); a page that still exceeds it is retried with half the page size. Our page size is capped at 100 (the portal allows 200). Tests run against `FakePortal`, which verifies the signature with its own implementation. | C1 confirmed; docs/CATALOG.md in the portal. |
| M2-18 | 2026-11-04 | **Mapping.** The Markdown `description` is used, never `descriptionHtml` (no portal HTML reaches our pages). Variants map with their resolved price; a listing with no variants becomes one "Standard" line on the listing price whose variant id is the **listing id** and which is stored with `IsDefault = true`: it is for display and cart only, and an order line for it must send `productId` with `variantId: null` (portal docs/ORDERS.md section 3: a line names `variantId` only when the product has variants), never the listing id as a variant id. M3 owns that change in order ingest. **Sale prices use OUR clock**: a sale applies only inside `[startsAt, endsAt)` as we judge it, whatever the portal's `active` says, and only to the listing's own price (a variant with its own price is not part of the sale; compare-at is shown only when it is above the price). Availability: `in_stock`, `low_stock`, `backorder`, `available` are buyable, `out_of_stock` or an unknown state is not; low stock and backorder also become an `availability` attribute. Extra fields land in the existing attribute and FAQ tables (no migration): `highlight` (bullets, 10), `returns-days`, `returns-condition`, `warranty-months`, `warranty-note`, `handling-days`, `pricing-model`, `duration`, `booking-mode`, `condition`; published questions with answers are appended to the FAQs (20), the asker is never in the feed. An attribute's unit is appended to its value. One document that cannot be read (a missing id, price, seller, brand or category) is logged by listing id and skipped; the cursor moves on. A single-listing fetch that cannot be read is an error (retried with backoff), never "removed". | docs/CATALOG.md sections 3, 9. |
| M2-19 | 2026-11-04 | **Delivery.** The explicit `delivery` object (C5) wins: `ships` gives `ships`, `localDelivery` gives `local`, and each of `serviceLocations` online / customer / seller gives `online` / `at-home` / `at-seller` (`at-seller` is new; the web label map ignores an unknown value until it learns it). An explicit all-false object means no delivery attribute. Only a document with no `delivery` object (an older portal) falls back to a service's own `online` / `atCustomer` / `atSeller` flags; products get nothing guessed. The M2-05 fixture still sets the attribute itself. | C5 (portal 75470d6). |
| M2-20 | 2026-11-04 | **Tombstones and search keywords.** A feed tombstone (`listed:false`) hides a listing we hold at its version and finishes it (nothing left to fetch); any reason, known or not (`moderated`, `archived`, `seller_suspended`, `brand_removed`, `not_active`, `deleted`, anything future), means "unlisted". A tombstone for a listing we never held creates nothing; an older one is ignored; a newer snapshot shows the listing again. The existing "portal answers 404 for a version we were told about means hidden" fallback stays. `searchTerms` are cut at 60 characters per term, 30 terms and 600 characters in all. Events were already deduplicated on the signed body `id` (M1: the header is checked against it, never trusted); the `listing.unlisted` handler never reads `reason`, and a test now sends `deleted` and an unknown one. Images: only ids and sizes are mapped; their bytes will be fetched through the signed client server-side only, by the `/img` route (later slice). | docs/CATALOG.md sections 2, 4. |
| M2-21 | 2026-11-04 | **Price cap for checkout (M3-22).** A variant whose unit price is above **99 999 999 cents** is stored as **not available** (`SnapshotLimits.MaxUnitPriceCents`), for every source (the sync applies it, so the fixture and the Http source both get it); the price is kept as sent and a warning with the listing id (never the content) is logged. The listing stays visible (it shows as sold out when no variant is buyable) rather than being hidden or its price clamped: a clamped price would show the buyer a wrong amount, and hiding would lose the page and its search position for a data problem the seller can fix. Exactly 99 999 999 is still buyable. Test: at the limit, one cent over, and a listing where only one variant is over. | Orchestrator relay of M3-22. |
| M2-22 | 2026-11-04 | **Image bytes** (`GET /api/v1/catalog/images/{id}`, `ImageEndpoints`, `ICatalogImageSource`). The read model only stored image ids and sizes; the web tier's resizer needs the original, so the API serves it from the portal (signed `GetCatalogImageAsync`: same signing, deadline and 503-retry rules as the JSON calls, body read as bytes with a 10 MB cap). **Only a visible listing's image is served, checked on every request before any cache** (an unlisted listing's images stop at once; unknown, malformed and hidden all give one 404 and the portal is not asked). **Only raster types are passed on** (`jpeg`, `png`, `webp`, `gif`, `avif`): the portal's stored type is trusted for nothing else, so an SVG or a document can never be served under our origin (it is a 502); every response also carries `nosniff`, `inline`, and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox`. Answers are kept in a bounded memory cache (64 MB, 10 min, owned by the singleton source). An outage is 503 with `Retry-After` and is never cached. With the seed fixture (no portal) a generated placeholder is served (made from the id alone, no catalog text). Tests: bytes and headers, 304, cache hit, unknown/unlisted-after-cached, every portal outcome, each allowed and refused type, oversize, empty, placeholder; 3 mutations checked. | The web `/img` route (W2-24). |
| M2-23 | 2026-11-04 | **Portal N3 (portal main 575b271): image key, deleted listings, dedupe, search terms.** (1) **Image key:** image downloads are signed with `Portal:ImageKey` when set, else with `Portal:InboundSecret` (fallback only while the portal still accepts it), and every other portal call keeps the inbound key. The key joins the start-up distinctness check (32+ characters, never equal to another key) and is optional until `Portal:RequireImageKey=true` (set it when the portal has `Orders:ImageAcceptInboundKey=false`; Production then refuses to start without it). Without it Production logs a warning at start-up (`StartupValidator.Warnings`; the text names the setting and never a key). Tests: which key signs which call (a portal that knows only one key accepts only its calls), the fallback, no key at all, optional/required, distinctness, short key, warnings, 2 mutations. (2) **Deleted listings:** a `listing.unlisted` event with reason `deleted` and a feed tombstone `{listed:false, reason:"deleted"}` both hide the listing (tests for both paths, M2-20; any reason is "unlisted"). (3) **Dedupe:** events are deduplicated on the signed body `id` (a different or missing `X-Solvelt-Event-Id` header is refused as `event_id_mismatch`, never trusted), and listings are ordered by `version` (a stale event or poll page cannot undo a newer state): both covered by existing receiver and catalog tests. (4) **searchTerms:** the portal now sends at most 20 terms of 50 characters and refuses emails and phone numbers; our limits (30 terms, 60 characters, 600 in total, M2-20) are a superset and stay. | Orchestrator relay of portal N3. |
| M2-24 | 2026-11-04 | **Portal C7 (portal main 3ed6c8b): weight and size.** `weightGrams` (int or null) maps to `catalog_variants.WeightGrams` (nullable int): a variant's own `weightGrams` when it is a positive whole number, else the product's; the synthetic default line of a variant-less listing carries the product's. `dimensionsMm {l,w,h}` maps to `catalog_listings.LengthMm/WidthMm/HeightMm` (nullable ints), stored only when all three sides are positive whole numbers, otherwise all three are null. **Unknown is null, never 0** (O-15): null, 0, negative, non-integer, non-numeric and out-of-range weights all become null. Both follow every new version (a changed weight is a new version in the portal), so a stale weight cannot mis-price shipping. There is no free-shipping flag in the portal and none is mapped. **For M1's InitialCreate:** `catalog_variants.WeightGrams` (int null), `catalog_listings.LengthMm`, `WidthMm`, `HeightMm` (int null each), next to `catalog_variants.IsDefault`. M3 reads `CatalogVariant.WeightGrams` (the `weight` attribute stays the fallback). | Orchestrator message, portal CATALOG.md C7. |
| M2-25 | 2026-11-05 | **The fixture placeholder image is a PNG, not an SVG.** With the seed fixture (no portal) the images endpoint served a generated SVG; the web `/img` resizer accepts raster pictures only (a seller's SVG is refused on purpose: M2-22, W2-25), so every fixture image answered 502 on a site running the fixture source (found in a real-browser run of the web against this API). The placeholder is now drawn with SkiaSharp (already a dependency, M5-22) as a PNG of the stored width and height, at most 1200 px a side, the same two colours from the image id, no catalog text. The raster-only wall stays as it was. Test `With_the_seed_fixture_the_placeholder_is_a_raster_picture_of_the_right_shape_made_from_the_id_alone` (decodes the PNG; mutants: labelled SVG again, wrong size, both fail). | Real-browser check of web against API, 2026-11-05. |
| M2-26 | 2026-11-05 | **Catalog review lows L2-L5 (no model change).** (L2) A feed tombstone with a listing id but an unreadable version (missing, zero, not a number) is no longer dropped while the cursor moves on (the listing would have stayed public): `HttpCatalogSource` turns it into a tombstone of `ListingTombstone.UnknownVersion` (0) and logs the id; `CatalogSync` hides the listing at the version it holds (`UnlistedVersion = Version`, idempotent), so any newer snapshot, a re-listing, shows it again. With no listing id there is nothing to hide: skipped as before. (L3) `SignedPortalClient.GetCatalogImageAsync` treats a whitespace `Portal:ImageKey` as not set, as the start-up validator does. (L4) The description is clipped to `SnapshotLimits.Description` (50 000 characters, never inside a surrogate pair) at ingest, in code only (no `HasMaxLength`, so no migration); the search index gets the clipped text. The web shows a description as plain text paragraphs (React-escaped, no Markdown and no raw HTML at all), tested against `<script>`, `<img onerror>`, `<b>` and Markdown syntax in the web E2E (W2-31). (L5) `ImageBytes` computes its strong ETag once when made (the instances live in the image cache), so a request never hashes the bytes; `If-None-Match: *` now answers 304 for a served image (an unknown image is still 404). No column. Tests: each is mutation-checked; the stale-tombstone case (an old tombstone after a re-listing leaves the row and a pending fetch unchanged) is new; the "complete Production config validates clean" test already exists (`FoundationTests.Production_with_complete_config_is_valid`). | Orchestrator review of f890812..f9aa676. |

## PII table
Every buyer-related field we store, why, how long, how it is redacted. Each slice extends this table.

| Field / data | Where | Why | Retention | Redaction |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Voice call metrics: masked caller (last four digits), model and tier, token counts, estimated cost, duration, latency, outcome, call id from SignalWire | `voice_calls` | Cost and quality comparison of the two voice models; matching SignalWire's callbacks | 400 days (`Voice:CallRetentionDays`), then deleted by the outbox worker | Holds no audio, transcript, address, card data or full number, and no account link: nothing to redact when an account is deleted |
| Phone number an account proved it holds (E.164) and when | `buyer_accounts.PhoneVerifiedE164`, `PhoneVerifiedAt` | Recognising a caller of the voice line as the account (V-05) | While the account exists; cleared when the profile phone changes or is removed | Erased with the account (redaction sets both to null) |
| Email address given by phone and not yet confirmed | `buyer_accounts.PendingEmail` | Mailing the confirmation link; becomes `Email` only when the link is confirmed (V-14) | Until the link is confirmed, replaced or expires (links last `Auth:VerificationTokenHours`; the address stays until replaced or the account is deleted) | Erased with the account (deletion and redaction set it to null) |
| Saved cards (V-16): the processor's token, brand, last four digits, expiry | `saved_cards` (`Token`, `Brand`, `Last4`, `ExpMonth`, `ExpYear`) | Paying later by phone with a card the account chose to save (a token is a credential for OUR Sola account only; never a card number or security code) | Until the account removes the card or is deleted | Deleted at once with the account (deletion and redaction); exported WITHOUT the token |
| Phone PIN (V-18): a peppered hash of the PIN, the wrong-try count and the lock time | `buyer_accounts.PinHash`, `PinFailures`, `PinLockedUntil` | A second factor for signing in by PHONE after the SMS code (never used on the web) | Until the caller removes the PIN or the account is deleted | Cleared with the account (deletion and redaction); never exported |
| A keyed hash of the caller's phone number | `voice_calls.CallerKey` | Counting phone orders per number per day (V-06) | With the call row (`Voice:CallRetentionDays`, 400 days) | A one-way hash under a key of its own; no account link; not reversible without the key |
| Payment attempt of a phone order: order number, method, amount, status, provider transaction reference | `voice_payment_attempts` | Settling a payment the provider reports (V-07) | `Voice:CallRetentionDays` (400 days), purged with the call rows | No personal data: no card, name or number |
| A keyed hash of the number that proved itself by SMS code in a call | `voice_calls.VerifiedKey` | Finding a guest's own phone orders in a later call (V-08) | With the call row (400 days) | A one-way hash under `Voice:CallerHashKey`; no account link |
| Quote request: job description, preferred times, address (lines, city, region, postal code, country, coordinates), phone | `quotes` | Getting a provider's quote; the portal receives the same | 30 days after the request ended (accepted, declined, withdrawn, expired), 1 day after a failed request (M4-13) | Text and address fields nulled/emptied, `RedactedAt` stamped; the quoted price, duration and ids stay |
| Booking: delivery address of an at-customer service (name, lines, city, region, postal code, country, coordinates), phone, intake answers | `bookings` | Providing the service; the portal receives the same | 30 days after the booking ended (completed, cancelled, declined, expired, no-show); 1 day after a failed hold attempt (M4-05); when the buyer deletes the account: finished bookings and quote requests at once, open ones when they finish (M4-18, M4-23, O-23) | Fields set to null, `RedactedAt` stamped; money, ids and statuses stay |
| Booking meeting link | `bookings.OnlineInstructions` | Joining an online service | Only while Confirmed, Rescheduled or InProgress; cleared when the booking leaves those states and by retention (M4-04) | Nulled; never in lists, never logged |
| Booking idempotency key and request hash | `bookings` | Making a double click or a retry one booking | With the row | Contain no personal data (a hash of the choices) |
| Booking payment and refund records: Stripe intent and refund ids, amounts, statuses, our idempotency keys | `booking_payments`, `booking_refunds` | Matching payments, idempotency, the refund cap, accounting | 7 years (as order payments) | Contain no personal data: Stripe holds the card |
| Raw portal webhook body (may contain: buyer-facing message text, driver first name, live driver position, proof URLs, relay identity, seller order numbers) | `portal_inbound_events.payload_json` | Replay of parked events; audit of what the portal sent | **at account deletion** for the account's events that are not parked (`PortalEventsAccountEraser`, M1-82; a parked one when it is finished, an event arriving after the deletion is never kept); otherwise 30 days; **24 h** for `order.tracking_updated` and `shipment.delivered` (positions and proof URLs, CONTRACT.md §5); a parked event until it is applied or **30 days** (`Portal:InboundParkedMaxAgeDays`), then it expires to `skipped` with the payload dropped (short types: 24 h) | Payload set to null and `payload_purged_at` set by the retention job; id, type, status and times remain until day 90, then the row is deleted |
| Portal event id, type, `createdAt`, status, aggregate type/key (marketplace order or booking id), `account_id` (the buyer the event is about, M1-82) | `portal_inbound_events` | Idempotency and replay guard, ordering, parking | 90 days (never deleted while parked) | Row deleted by the retention job |
| Aggregate key, last-applied time, terminal state | `projection_states` | Ordering and terminal-state rules | With the aggregate it describes (extended when the order/booking read models land) | Deleted with the aggregate; contains no buyer data itself |
| Catalog data (listings, variants, images, attributes, FAQs, categories, brands, search words) and the sync cursor | `catalog_*` (incl. `catalog_search`) | Public marketplace content | While the portal lists it; a listing the portal removes is hidden and kept as a tombstone row | Contains no buyer data |
| Seller display name (can be a person's name) and active flag | `catalog_sellers` | Seller cards, brand pages, suspended-seller handling | While the seller has a visible listing and **90 days** after the last time it did (`Catalog:SellerRetentionDays`) | Row deleted by the sync run; a returning seller is re-created by its next snapshot. Never logged (log lines carry ids only; EF error logs are silenced, M2-12) |
| Email message to send (recipient address, subject, body, which can hold a verification or reset link) | `outbox_messages.payload` | Reliable delivery of buyer mail | Payload removed 1 day after delivery, 30 days after dead-lettering; row (id, type, status, timing, error code) deleted at 90 days | Payload set to NULL by the maintenance job; never logged; never returned by the admin endpoints |

| M3-25 | 2026-11-04 | **Independent review of the one-pending-order work (F1-F5).** (F1) `StripeCancelHandler` is *Delivered* only when the intent is verifiably closed: cancelled now, `resource_missing`, or `payment_intent_unexpected_state` with Stripe status `succeeded`/`canceled`; in flight (`processing`, `requires_capture`) it retries, anything else (a rotated key ...) is *Dead* (visible). The purge of an expired/superseded order requires its cancel row to be Delivered (`cancel:<intent>`, or `cancel-orphan:<number>` for an order that never stored an intent), so a dead or pending cancel keeps the order for a person. A `payment_intent.succeeded` for an intent that carries our order number in its metadata but has no matching live order (purged, or another intent is stored) is refunded by intent id through the outbox (`stripe.refund-intent`, key derived from the intent, idempotent) instead of being acknowledged and kept; the event outcome is `applied`. (F2) Expiry runs per order in ONE transaction: status change, then the intent id is read, then the cancel row is queued, so a crash loses nothing and an intent stored after the listing is still cancelled. (F3, **O-19**) Intents are `payment_method_types[]=card` (cards and wallets; no ACH/SEPA at launch). Before a different checkout supersedes an order, Stripe is asked for the intent's status (outside the transaction): `processing`/`requires_capture`, or an order already `payment_processing`, answers `409 checkout_in_progress` and nothing is closed. `payment_intent.processing` marks the order `payment_processing`, which supersede and expiry leave alone; success pays it, `payment_failed` makes it payable again. (F4) Closing an order with NO stored intent queues `stripe.cancel-orphan`: it asks Stripe again with the SAME key `pi:<number>` (returns the intent, or creates one) and cancels it; the intent_mismatch branch cancels the stray intent. (F5) `CheckoutHooks.BeforeSupersedeUpdate` lets a test store an intent between supersede's listing and its status change (deterministic; catches a read-before-update mutant on SQLite); the comment on the cart row lock no longer claims it is what prevents two payable orders (the unique indexes and the retry do; the lock only queues identical checkouts on PostgreSQL). Tests changed on purpose: payment on an intent no live order stores is refunded (was `unmatched`), purge waits for the delivered cancel. 9 new tests in `PendingHardeningTests`; mutants (cancel handler delivers everything / purge ignores the cancel) fail them. Not mutation-tested: F2 ordering (design + test), F5 hook. | Orchestrator review of 8912fb2. |
| M3-26 | 2026-11-04 | **Dispute shares for C10 (portal 3ee6752, PAYMENTS.md "Disputes on multi-seller orders").** One `dispute.created\|updated\|closed` per seller order of the payment, each with the same `disputeId`, `marketplaceOrderId`, its own `sellerOrderNumber` (always sent: the portal answers 422 `seller_order_required` without it for multi-seller orders), its share `amountCents` and `feeCents`, and `status`/`createdAt`/`evidenceDueBy`; updated and closed (won/lost) go out for EVERY seller order. **Allocation (`StripeEventProcessor.AllocateDispute`):** (1) split the Stripe amount in proportion to each group's CHARGED total (tax included), floor, the remainder to the largest group; (2) cap each share at what that seller order SOLD (items + shipping, tax excluded: the portal answers 422 `exceeds_sale` above it); (3) what a cap cuts goes to the groups that still have room, largest room first, so the shares add up to min(amount, sum of sales); never negative. The part that is tax and cannot be placed stays with the portal's "unallocated dispute" staff flag, as agreed. **Fee:** when Stripe's dispute object carries balance transactions with a fee, the fee is split by the same charged-total proportion (floor, remainder to the largest) and sent as `feeCents` on every event; without it the old behaviour stays (first seller order says nothing so the portal default applies once, the others 0). A single-seller dispute is now capped at the order's sale (14000 charged back on a 14262 order whose seller sold 13100 sends 13100; it was 12859 by proportion: changed on purpose). A rejected share (422) is dead in our outbox and visible to a person; the other seller orders' events are independent and delivered. Replays are unchanged (event id dedupe, `dispute_states` order guard). Held events are still released only by `Portal:PerSellerOrderDisputes`; the default flips in the next slice once this is merged. Tests: allocation unit cases (2-seller split, cap binding, cut moved to room, full dispute, degenerate input, 2000 random shapes for the invariants), a partial multi-seller dispute with fee and one refused share. | Orchestrator note on C10 (3ee6752). |
| M3-27 | 2026-11-04 | **Review of c996c61..f82c025 (C1-C5, survivors) and the portal's N8 refund semantics.** (C1) Removing a cart line or changing its quantity runs in ONE transaction that starts with the cart's lock (it used to lock outside any transaction, so the lock was gone at once): a sign-in merge waits for the write or has finished, in which case the lock finds no cart and the answer is `404`, never a `200` for a change that was lost, and a statement that touches no row is a 404 too. `CartHooks.AfterLineRead` pauses it right after the lock for a test. Honest limit: on SQLite (one writer at a time) the test cannot tell the old no-transaction code from the new, so the guarantee is proven on the PostgreSQL CI job, not by a mutant here. (C2/C3, brief N8, portal 3a6ea30) With `Portal:PerGroupShipping` on, `refund.issued` and `order.cancelled` carry `shippingTaxCents` as an explicit field and `amountCents` = lines (with their tax) + `shippingCents` (net) + `shippingTaxCents`; `taxCents` = line tax + shipping tax. The explicit figure is used as sent (capped at what is left of the group's shipping tax, and never more than the amount has room for); only when it is missing (an older portal) is it inferred: proportional to the shipping refunded, the last shipping refund (or a full cancel) taking the remainder. A refund with no lines first takes the shipping tax out of the gross and the remaining line tax before prorating (it counted it twice: tax 133 instead of 118). The shipping tax that does not fit inside the amount is NOT counted (RefundedShippingTaxCents used to drift to 18 while the tax refunded was 0); ShippingTaxCents is always part of TaxCents. A payload whose lines + shipping + shipping tax do not equal the amount is not acted on. `refund.processed` stays tax-exclusive. (C4) A group that ships free (items subtotal at or over the threshold) offers weight-based speeds at 0 even when a weight is unknown; below the threshold an unknown weight still makes them unavailable. The O-15 test was changed on purpose for this (its group was over the threshold). (C5) `quoteHash` is required on every checkout (a missing or null one is `409 price_changed` with the fresh quote; tested). Survivors: M19 (uneven 333/167 split of a 45 shipping tax: 29 then 16), M21 (the clamp: an amount with no room for it), M22 (a Stripe refusal that moved no money gives RefundedShippingTaxCents back too; fake `RejectRefundWith`), M23 (a webhook-level refund with the switch on and the portal's explicit shippingTaxCents). Mutants fail: no clamp, no remainder, no give-back. | Orchestrator review of c996c61..f82c025 and brief N8. |
| M3-28 | 2026-11-04 | **Refund reconciliation against Stripe-confirmed money (review R1, R2, R1c).** The old rule ("a refusal for `charge_exceeds_amount` / `charge_already_refunded` means the money is gone, keep the reservation") was based on error codes only the fake provider invents and it stranded money: a dashboard refund our webhook had not processed yet made the order's counter (which includes reservations Stripe never executed) say `refunded` while the buyer had only the dashboard's part, and the late `charge.refunded` was then ignored. Now `StripeRefundHandler`, on ANY refusal, reads what Stripe has really refunded (`GetAmountRefundedAsync`: the intent's latest charge `amount_refunded`; a fresh read, never the error code) and: (1) records the part we did not know (confirmed - our succeeded refunds - known external) as an `external` refund and adds it to the order's counter; (2) computes what can still go back (`PaidCents - confirmed - the other reserved refunds`); (3) a refund that fits is simply refused (its counters, units and tax given back, dead message, as before); one that no longer fits is CUT to what is left and re-sent under a new idempotency attempt (`refund:<key>:<n>`, outbox row `refund:<key>:<n>`; the order's and the seller order's counters shrink by the difference, the units the portal named stay refunded, a log error says so); with nothing left it is given back entirely and the cap stays consumed by the confirmed external money instead of by a reservation. Stripe not knowing the intent (`resource_missing`) means no money left: given back. **The buyer mail of a failed order** ("X is being refunded") is no longer sent on reservation: `StripeRefundHandler` sends it in the same transaction as the success, with the amount really returned ("in full" / "the remaining X"), or "everything has already been returned" when nothing was owed or nothing could go. Tests changed on purpose: the two "money is gone keeps its reservation" tests now expect the counters to follow the confirmed money (order RefundedCents = PaidCents through an `external` row, the refused refund's units given back). New: unprocessed dashboard refund + rejected order (refund 300 dashboard + the true remainder, mail once, late webhook changes nothing), portal refund cut and re-sent (9174 -> 8262 after a 6000 dashboard refund), no mail while only reserved. Integration note: real Stripe test mode reports an over-refund as an `invalid_request_error` whose code is not `charge_exceeds_amount` (a message about the refund being greater than the unrefunded amount); the new logic does not depend on the code. The provider read needs the secret key only (`expand[]=latest_charge`); to be verified against Stripe test mode once `STRIPE_TEST_SECRET_KEY` is available in CI. | Orchestrator review of 8912fb2..c996c61 (R1, R2). |
| M3-29 | 2026-11-04 | **Out-of-order portal and Stripe events (review R3, R4, R5, R8 purge; portal answers on C14 and C10).** (R3) `order.cancelled` can overtake an earlier `refund.issued` (a failed delivery backs off; only listing events are ordered today). A FULL cancel is parked (and replayed by the existing parked-event loop) while what the portal says it already returned (`previouslyRefundedCents` once the portal sends it: brief N9 / C14; until then `TotalCents - refundDueCents`) is more than what we applied to that seller order; it applies once the earlier refunds have, and a log error says so after 30 minutes of waiting. `refundIdsAccounted` is not used yet. (R4/R5) The dispute guard is ONE conditional write (insert on conflict do nothing for the first event, then `UPDATE ... WHERE NOT Closed AND (LastCreated < @t OR (LastCreated = @t AND LastEventId < @id))`, rows must be 1), so parallel events never overwrite each other and two events of the same second have a stable order (larger Stripe event id wins; new `LastEventId`, `UpdatedAt` columns). Nothing follows a closed dispute. A `created` is NEVER dropped for arriving after a later event (it is sent; the state keeps the newer time); updated/closed for a (dispute, seller order) the portal has not seen is an upsert there, so the first event we send always carries the amount and the fee and no synthetic created is needed; an older UPDATE is dropped. `dispute_states` rows are purged 90 days after the last event when closed, 180 otherwise (it was promised in M3-19 and missing). Every dispute event carries the ORDER's currency, never Stripe's (portal 422 currency_mismatch); a won on a multi-seller order is sent for every seller order (the portal credits the seller only through our event). Tests: late created, closed then stale created, same-second tie, 8 parallel events, currency, purge. Because a buyer could still press cancel/return/confirm/message/report/reschedule on a demo row (which WOULD queue work), those routes refuse demo rows with **409 `demo_data`** through endpoint filters that run after sign-in (`DemoOrderGuard`, `DemoBookingGuard`, `DemoMessageGuard`), and the three maintenance selectors that call a payment provider or the portal (`OrderMaintenance.ExpirePending`, the two in `BookingMaintenance`) exclude demo rows explicitly (tested with an aged demo row next to an aged real one). The refusal is part of the one conditional UPDATE (no check-then-write gap) and is logged. Not done: restricting verify to accounts created while demo mode was on (it needs a column; demo mode is off in Production anyway and the address check closes the hole). (M2) *Remove never deletes real data.* `CartLine.IsDemo` is new (**model change**, told to M1 for InitialCreate; bool, not null, default false): Fill marks its lines, Remove deletes only marked lines of the account's cart; a cart left with real lines is the buyer's own (`IsDemo` and batch cleared), one left empty is deleted. A buyer who edits a demo address (PUT) makes it their own: `IsDemo` and the batch are cleared, so Remove leaves it. (L1) the mixed-account Remove test now has a whole real order (group, line, seller state, shipment, line fulfilment, refund) and a real cart elsewhere, and asserts they survive. (L2) `DemoBookingGuard` now also covers extra-charge accept and decline and the booking checkout; `DemoMessageGuard` also reads `SellerOrderNumber` and `MarketplaceBookingId`. (L3) `/api/v1/demo/{**rest}` is a catch-all 404, so a wrong method or unknown path under `/demo` is a 404, never a 405, while the feature is off. (L4) Fill and Remove share a per-account limit of 10 per minute (`DemoActionLimiter`, 429 with Retry-After; counters live in a private memory cache with an expiry, so nothing grows without bound), and the static per-account lock dictionary is a fixed set of 64 locks. (L5) A failed or throttled verify is logged with a 12-character hash of the client address (never the email or password), identically for known and unknown addresses. Every guard has a test that fails when it is removed (mutation-checked). | Orchestrator, M9 security review. |
| M9-06 | 2026-11-05 | **Demo mode is allowed only on an allowlist of environments (supersedes M9-03's denylist).** `Demo:Enabled=true` starts only when `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` is exactly Development, Testing or Staging (trimmed, case-insensitive); every other name refuses (`Production`, `Prod`, ` Production`, `Live`, `Anything`, `Demo`, a misspelling of Staging, blank or unset), with no override. M9-03 refused only `IsProduction()` and let any other name through, so a mistyped production name would have started with demo data on. This is a deliberate tightening, not a weakening: `DemoFoundationTests` now asserts refusal for "Anything", "Prod", " Production", "Live" and the rest, and acceptance for the three names. The web keeps its own refusal by deployment name (W2-29); it is a second switch behind this one (the web shows the buttons only when the API says `demoEnabled`), so an API that refuses to start with demo on keeps the buttons off. | Orchestrator, M9 review (ALSO). |
| M9-07 | 2026-11-05 | **Demo verification is recorded, and the rest of the review's round 2.** (HIGH-1) `BuyerAccount.DemoVerifiedAt` (**model change**, InitialCreate regenerated with `tools/migrations/regenerate-initial-create.sh`; only the one column differs): the test-only verify sets `EmailVerifiedAt` (so the account can sign in) AND `DemoVerifiedAt` in the one conditional update. While it is set the account never receives a guest order, on ANY path: the guard is inside `GuestOrderClaimer`'s single UPDATE (`NOT EXISTS` an account with that id and `DemoVerifiedAt` set), which sign-in, passkey sign-in, verification and reset all end in. Closing the second half of H1: an attacker who signs up as `victim@x` and demo-verifies while there are no guest orders cannot claim the order the victim places as a guest later. A REAL mailbox proof (the verification link, a password reset) clears `DemoVerifiedAt` before it claims, so that account then receives its orders as any other. Tests: verify, guest order placed later, sign in three times (and the claimer called directly): unclaimed; link and reset clear the marker and claim; an ordinary account still claims. (MED-3) The cart's three raw upserts and `SetQuantityAsync` set `IsDemo` to false: a buyer who adds the same variant, changes the quantity of a demo line, or merges a guest cart onto one owns those units, so Remove no longer deletes them (tests for add, quantity and merge). (LOW-10) The sign-in gate is cleared by a demo verify only after its conditional update succeeded or the account was already verified: a REFUSED verify counts against the address like a wrong password and no longer resets the counter (test: repeated refused verifies reach 429). | Orchestrator review of d6fb650..9e8db2f (HIGH-1, MED-3, LOW-10). |
| M9-08 | 2026-11-05 | **Last M9 round (independent review of 2143e48: demo mode is safe on a testers-only Staging site; these make it safe with real guest checkouts).** (MED-1) The account-deletion hook (`OrdersAccountEraser.OnDeletingAsync`) no longer nulls `GuestEmail` on unclaimed guest orders when the DELETED account is demo-verified (`DemoVerifiedAt` set, the same guard as the claimer, inside the same single UPDATE): a squatter who signed up as `victim@x`, demo-verified and later deleted the account used to make the real owner's guest orders unreachable (no shipping or refund mail, never claimable) and could repeat it for every new order. An ordinary account still strips its own address (control in the test). (LOW-1) `CartView.HasDemoLines` (not serialised) is computed from the very lines `CartService.BuildAsync` reads, and `QuoteService` refuses on it right after `GetAsync` (the separate `HasDemoLinesAsync` query is gone: no gap between "is it a demo cart" and "what is in it"); checkout asks again INSIDE its transaction, under the cart lock, before the order is inserted (`DemoCartException` -> `409 demo_data`), so a fill racing a checkout can never produce an order for demo goods. Test seam `CheckoutHooks.AfterQuote`; test: a demo line arriving after the quote is refused, no order, no intent. (LOW-2) A reset through the mailbox on a demo-verified account now also clears the display name ("Buyer"), the phone and every saved address (what whoever registered the address typed is not kept for its real owner), like it already did for an unverified account; only the reset branch of `AuthService` changed. (LOW-3) `Demo:Enabled` is read by ONE function, `DemoOptions.Parse` (`bool.TryParse`, which also trims control characters, so `true\0` is true), used by the application AND the start-up validator, so they cannot disagree (the validator compared the string `"true"` while the binder accepted `true\0`: a production start with `true\0` was not refused and demo was ON). A non-blank value that is not a boolean refuses the start in every environment; blank is off. Found on the way: the configuration binder THROWS on a blank value, so an empty `Demo__Enabled=` (normal in a container template) made `/api/v1/config` and every demo route answer 500; the options are now set from `DemoOptions.Parse`, not bound (test: blank starts and says `demoEnabled:false`). (LOW-4) A demo cart whose lines the buyer has all adopted (the cart row still says demo) can be checked out: the lines decide. (INFO, decision: NOT done) Mail for demo-verified accounts is not suppressed: the sources are many and belong to other modules (notifications, auth, the Stripe and booking paths) and the account exists only where demo mode is allowed (Development, Testing, Staging, M9-06); a test site must send its mail to a sandbox (Mailpit, a catch-all) and never to real addresses, now stated in the README. No model change. Every new guard is mutation-checked (9 mutants). | Orchestrator review of 2143e48. |

| FX1-01 | 2026-11-17 | **Parallel hold with one idempotency key answered 500 (CI run 42269731980, SQLite).** Suspected cause (not reproduced locally: no .NET SDK in the worker sandbox; CI is the runner): `BookingService.HoldAsync` caught every `DbUpdateException` on the insert of the `HoldPending` row and re-read the row by (account, key); when the write failed on a lock (SQLite busy/locked) instead of the unique index, no row existed and the code rethrew, so the request got a 500. A double-click could hit it too. Fix: the insert is retried (12 attempts, short growing random pause) when the failure is retryable by the shared rule `AccountService.IsRetryable` (unique violation, SQLite 5/6, PostgreSQL 40001/40P01), the winner's row is still answered when one exists, a different request hash is still 409, and after the attempts the buyer gets `TooBusyException` (503 + Retry-After), never a 500. This is the pattern the address book already uses. Tests (`BookingHoldRaceTests`): an interceptor injects a busy error on the first inserts (fails without the fix), a never-freeing lock is a 503, and 20 rounds of 12 parallel holds print the exception behind any 500. The original test is unchanged. **Second cause, found by the 20-round loop on PostgreSQL (CI run 44179002888, round 19: four 422s, no 500s):** a same-key request that looked its key up BEFORE the winner's insert committed counted the open holds AFTER it, saw the cap (the 20th hold of the loop; `MaxActiveHolds` is clamped to 20) and answered 422 `too_many_holds` instead of replaying the winner's hold: a double-click on a buyer's last allowed hold got a false refusal. Fix: when the cap check fails, the row for (account, key) is read again; if it exists, the request-hash check and the replay apply, and only otherwise is it `too_many_holds`. The loop stays as it is (its 20th hold is exactly at the cap, which is below the refusal point, so it does not exceed the cap for unrelated reasons) and `BookingHoldCapRaceTests` forces the interleaving deterministically through the `IHoldRaceProbe` seam in `BookingService.HoldAsync` (no-op in production; points BeforeCapCount, BeforeInsert, AfterInsert: the first cap count passes, later ones wait for the first insert, the first insert waits for a parked request; the test asserts the interleaving happened. A first attempt with EF command interceptors never fired, because EF counts through a data reader, not ExecuteScalar) with `MaxActiveHolds` 3 and two holds already open; a genuinely new hold over the cap is still 422. |
